## Lesson: one shared test database, two agents

B1 and F1 ran concurrently against the same `licitaqui_test` database. B1's unfiltered
`claim()` picked up ~100 job rows created by F1's signup tests and flipped them to
`running`. B1 caught it (its race test claimed rows it had not created) and fixed it
properly: every database test now uses a job kind of its own (`b1t_<uuid>`) and claims
with that filter. The table was left empty.

That was an orchestration mistake, not an agent mistake — handing two parallel agents the
same database invites it. Either give each task its own database, or require a
task-scoped `kind` prefix in the brief. It also produced a genuinely useful production
API: `claim(conn, kinds=[...])` and `WORKER_JOB_KINDS`, so a container can be dedicated
to part of the queue.

## Integration checks (2026-09-17, live)

| Service | Result |
|---|---|
| OpenRouter | ✅ valid, usage 0, **spend limit 50** (gap G4 satisfied) |
| Telegram | ✅ `@LicitaQuiBot`, **no webhook set** — gap G9's worry is clear, E1 can claim it |
| Neon | ✅ both pooled and direct URLs connect |
| Evolution API | ✅ reachable, instance `flowdeski-scn-real-estate` state `open` — **but see the two warnings below** |
| Resend, Asaas, Sentry, Google, S3 | not configured yet — blocks E-mail, F2, observability, U1, B4 respectively |

**Evolution warning 1 — Cloudflare blocks default HTTP clients.** `evolutiondev.scintechn.com`
sits behind Cloudflare, which answers `403 error code: 1010` (browser-signature block) to
`Python-urllib`, *including on `GET /`*. It is not an auth failure and no API key will fix
it. **E2's client must send a browser-like `User-Agent`**, or every WhatsApp send fails 403.
Verified: identical request with a Chrome UA returns 200.

**Evolution warning 2 — the instance belongs to another product.** The only instance on
that server is `flowdeski-scn-real-estate`, i.e. FlowDeski's. Sending LicitaQui's founders
welcome through it would deliver from another product's WhatsApp number. This is the same
trap gap G9 flags for the Telegram bot. **Recommend a dedicated `licitaqui` instance before
E2 sends anything**, even to test contacts.

## G7 re-measurement in progress (overnight 09-17 → 09-18)

The hourly probe ADR-0001 asked for is running. Early result, and it is not reassuring:

| Sample (UTC) | search | publicacao | atualizacao |
|---|---|---|---|
| 21:21 | 2/2 OK (~1.87 s) | **4/4 timeout** | **4/4 timeout** |
| 22:25 | 2/2 OK | **4/4 timeout** | **4/4 timeout** |
| direct one-off, 45 s timeout | inconclusive (different UA) | **2/2 timeout** | **2/2 timeout** |

The `consulta` host has been timing out for **over an hour** while the search host answers
normally. Every failure is a read timeout, the same signature B0 saw in its 13-minute
outage. ADR-0001's inversion rule is >10% failures over a day, or outages beyond ~2 h.

**RESOLVED by B2's verification — the endpoint choice stands, the cadence is what to
revisit.** B2 answered the open question: the search index's `data_atualizacao_pncp` does
**not** move when only items or files change. It is byte-identical to
`data_publicacao_pncp`, sub-second digits included. Evidence: of 95 cached tenders, **79
(83.2%) had a child change**; of those, **74 matched the header timestamp exactly and 0
matched the global one**. A search-only sweep would have missed all 74 **silently**.

That inverts the inversion rule. The outage is a *freshness* problem, which the queue and
breaker already absorb; falling back to search as the primary would be a *correctness*
problem, and a silent one. **Ignore my earlier hybrid recommendation** — it was written
before this evidence, and search-as-reconciliation cannot detect child changes either, so
it would only catch tenders missed entirely. Revisit cadence and retry policy, not the
endpoint.

**Outage record, for the ADR:** `atualizacao` timed out again
at 00:35 (40 s), while the search host answered in 0.7 s. That is **~3 h 14 min of
continuous timeouts** on the consulta host, against ADR-0001's ">2 h outage" trigger.

The maintenance-window explanation is now the weaker one: 21:21–00:35 UTC is
**18:21–21:35 in Brazil** — peak evening, not a maintenance slot. B0 also recorded the
same host failing at 20:21–20:34 UTC, so the disruption has run since roughly 20:21 UTC
with at most brief recovery.

Six sync cycles were missed. What this costs is delay, not data, because the watermark
only advances when a cycle completes every modality — a partial cycle is retried whole.

**What still needs deciding** is narrower than it looked: how long a consulta outage may
last before it is an incident rather than a delay, and whether a same-day outage should
raise anything in `/admin`. That is O2/observability work, not an architecture fork.

## Two defects found by B5, both worth fixing

1. **CI does not check Python formatting.** `ci-worker.yml` runs `ruff check .` but not
   `ruff format --check .`, so drift lands on `main` unnoticed — it already had, in B0's
   two probe scripts. B5's PR reformats them. **Add the format check to `ci-worker.yml`
   once #12 merges** (doing it before would put `main` red).
2. **B1's integration tests are not isolated between concurrent runs.** Confirmed
   independently by B5 and B2; B2 reproduced it against `main`'s own code with no B2 work
   in the session (2 failures in run 1, 0 in runs 2–3). **It will keep producing spurious
   red CI runs until someone owns it**, and a test suite that cries wolf is worse than one
   that is merely slow.

   Root cause, in `worker/tests/conftest.py`: the cleanup comment says deletes are "scoped
   by prefix", but the prefixes are module **constants** —

   ```python
   KEY_PREFIX = "b1-test-"
   KIND_PREFIX = "b1t_"
   ...
   delete from jobs where starts_with(key, %s) or starts_with(kind, %s)
   ```

   so they scope by *task*, not by *run*. Two concurrent runs of the same suite delete
   each other's fixtures mid-test. My per-agent databases (`TEST_DATABASE_URL_B2/_O1/_B5`)
   isolated the *new* tests but not these inherited ones.

   Fix — make the prefix per-run, ~3 lines:
   ```python
   RUN_ID = uuid.uuid4().hex[:8]
   KEY_PREFIX = f"b1-test-{RUN_ID}-"
   KIND_PREFIX = f"b1t_{RUN_ID}_"
   ```
   **Order matters:** both PR #12 and PR #13 already modify `conftest.py`, so do this
   *after* they merge or it conflicts with two green PRs. That is why it is written down
   here instead of already done.

   Third time a shared test database has caused a problem. Worth a standing rule: every
   test that writes to a shared database scopes its rows by a per-run id, not a per-task
   constant.

## Gap G6 closed (2026-09-18) — pending Sci's review

B6 (PR #16) builds the CNAE → segment map, so "Compatível / Verificar" is computable for
the first time. **572 mappings over 555 of the 1,332 IBGE subclasses; 777 deliberately
unmapped.** Unmapped is not `check`: it means the activity is outside the fourteen
segments, and the Radar falls back to keyword search.

It reversed its own design rule on measurement, which is the part worth trusting:

| Rule | `compatible` per company | 20-CNPJ result |
|---|---|---|
| strongest claim wins (incl. secondaries) | mean 5.75 of 14 | 16/20 |
| **main CNAE decides** (shipped) | mean 0.8 | **19/20** |

A small company's secondary CNAEs are whatever its accountant registered, so letting them
grant `compatible` made almost everything compatible. Secondaries still contribute the
segment at `check`.

The single miss was the agent's own expectation being wrong, not the map — and it said so
rather than adjusting the expectation to fit.

**Sci owns the 150-code review** (plan §5). Start with `db/reference/cnae_segments.csv`,
and focus where B6 says its judgement has no data behind it: the goods-versus-services
line (`8121400` *limpeza em prédios* and health divisions 86–87 are `check` — if concierge
tenders are mostly cleaning *services*, that should flip), retail 47 / wholesale 46 where
MEI/ME actually live, and the six catch-all codes left unmapped on purpose.

## Still to do after B3 and B6 land

- **Finish the per-run scoping.** PR #14 made `conftest.py`'s `jobs` prefixes `RUN_ID`-based
  but missed the other half: B2's sync tests scope `tenders` rows by a **constant CNPJ**
  (`99000000000102`), so two concurrent runs of `test_integration_sync_tenders.py` still
  delete each other's rows. C1 hit it live while B3 was running the same file. Same defect,
  different table — my fix was incomplete. The rule needs to cover **natural keys**, not
  just job keys.
- **Set `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` as a repo secret.** Without it `evaluate-ai.yml` runs in
  replay against committed recordings — it passes, and the report says `REPLAYED`, but the
  gate is not actually exercising the model. A live run costs about R$ 0,004.
- **Document `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` and `TEST_DATABASE_URL_C1` in `.env.example`** — a deny
  rule blocks agents from that file.

## The screening path is still broken end to end (found 2026-09-18 by FH)

`requestScreening` enqueues `{tender_id}` and nothing else. `load_document` requires one
of `pages` / `text_path` / `pdf_path` / `url` and raises `ValueError` otherwise, so the
queued job fails four times and lands `failed`. **A user clicking "analisar" today gets
nothing.**

This is not a defect in C1, B4, R1 or FH — each is correct within its card. It is the
seam none of them owned: B4's card says "list only; **download on demand**", and the
on-demand half was never carded. `tender_files.url` is populated and waiting.

What the missing card has to do: fetch the PDF for a tender's active documents, extract
the text (`pdfplumber`, already a worker dependency), store it per §3.2 — S3 for the PDF,
text in the database, delete the PDF 90 days after closing — and hand `load_document`
what it expects. The seam is `ai_screening.resolve_files_hash`, which already reads the
active list at exactly the right moment.

Two related gaps worth folding in:
- `readScreening` ignores `prompt_version`, so a prompt rollback can still serve a row the
  worker would not consider current.
- B4's digest covers the file **list**, not the bytes. An agency replacing a file at the
  same URL without moving `dataPublicacaoPncp` stays invisible until `tender_files.sha256`
  is folded in under `MANIFEST_VERSION` 2. The seam is ready.

## Open for Sci

- **Event name mismatch.** Spec §14 names the event `founder_signed_up`; F1 writes
  `founder_signup`. O1 kept F1's stored name (changing it was out of its scope) and
  catalogued both. Decide: rename the event and backfill, or amend §14 to match the code.
  Cheap now — a handful of rows — and it only gets more expensive once the gate queries
  and the digest depend on it. Not urgent: the founders gate counts `founders_list` rows
  directly, so the number is right either way.
- **Concierge-paying gate has no source.** §14 lists it among the six Phase 0 gate
  numbers, but no table marks the concierge cohort. `/admin` renders "sem fonte" rather
  than a fabricated 0/20. Needs either a column or a documented manual count before the
  11-06 gate review.

## Decisions (2026-09-17)

- **Auth:** Auth.js / NextAuth v5 stands (spec §5). The `neon_auth` schema the Vercel
  integration provisioned is unused; consider dropping it before launch.
- **Founders form:** **CNPJ is required**, because it determines which licitações a
  business can enter. "O que você vende" (`sells`) stays on the form but is **optional**.
  This makes the rendered form differ from the approved HTML, deliberately.
- **Input font size:** form inputs render at **16px**, not the board's 15px. iOS Safari
  zooms the viewport on focus for anything smaller, which is a real defect on a public
  page opened from a phone. Documented in `components/field.tsx`.
- **i18n duplicates:** where E0's catalogue and D2's page disagree, the approved page's
  rendered wording wins and E0's key naming wins. Reconciled in F1.
- **PNCP sync endpoint (G7):** `/v1/contratacoes/atualizacao`, not the POC search API. The
  search API caps `pagina × tam_pagina` at 10,000 against ~38,856 open tenders and silently
  ignores date filters, so a sweep cannot enumerate everything and misses changes silently;
  an outage on the period endpoint merely delays a cycle. **Inversion rule:** flip back if a
  full-day re-measurement shows >10% failures or outages beyond ~2 h. **Open question for
  B2's first hour:** does the search index's `data_atualizacao_pncp` move when only a
  tender's items or files change? If yes, the correctness argument weakens and search-only
  may win.
- **Branch protection:** not available on a private repo without GitHub Pro. Gap accepted
  — `gitleaks` still runs on every PR, advisory only. Revisit before anyone else commits.

## A2 — what was applied to Neon (2026-09-17)

Neon project `neon-bistre-cloud` (`rapid-mouse-53141905`), São Paulo, database `neondb`,
**PostgreSQL 18.6** (spec §5 says 16 — newer, all required extensions available).

Applied to the **main** branch, with Sci's explicit go-ahead:
- Extensions: `unaccent`, `pg_trgm`, `citext`.
- Role `migrator` — DDL: `USAGE, CREATE ON SCHEMA public`.
- Role `app` — DML only: `USAGE ON SCHEMA public`, `SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE` on all
  tables, `USAGE, SELECT` on sequences, plus `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES FOR ROLE migrator`
  so tables created by migrations are automatically reachable.

Verified, not assumed: `migrator` created a table, `app` did DML on it, `app` was **denied**
`CREATE TABLE`, `unaccent('licitação') = 'licitacao'`, `pg_trgm` similarity and `citext`
case-insensitive compare all behave. Test table dropped afterwards.

Credentials are in `.env.neon-roles.local` (gitignored, mode 600) — never committed.

**Decision (2026-09-17):** the Vercel integration provisioned **Neon Auth** (9 tables in a
`neon_auth` schema). Sci confirmed spec §5 stands: **Auth.js / NextAuth v5**. The
`neon_auth` schema is unused and left untouched; consider dropping it before launch.

## Notes

- The repository lives at `/Users/sci/Claude/Projects/LicitaQui`, **not** inside the
  knowledge-base folder as the original `CLAUDE.md` §5 assumed. The pointer in `CLAUDE.md`
  is therefore an absolute path to `/Users/sci/Documents/POC Licitacao`.
- `pnpm-workspace.yaml` declares `allowBuilds: {unrs-resolver: true}` so that
  `pnpm install` never stops for approval in CI or on Vercel. pnpm 11 does **not** read
  `onlyBuiltDependencies`, and `pnpm rebuild` writes an unresolved placeholder into that
  file — verify a clean `rm -rf node_modules && pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` before
  pushing changes to it.
- Commit identity is pinned in `CLAUDE.md`. Getting it wrong does more than mis-attribute:
  a commit author who is not an approved team member makes Vercel **refuse to build at
  all**, with no build logs to explain it.
- **Regions.** Neon is in São Paulo (`aws-sa-east-1`, permanent). Vercel functions are
  therefore pinned to `gru1` in `apps/web/vercel.json` (spec §5.1). Note the *build*
  region is chosen by Vercel and may still show `iad1` — that is the build machine, not
  where functions execute, and it does not affect query latency.
- `vercel.json` lives in `apps/web/`, not the repo root, because the project's Root
  Directory is `apps/web`.
- **Frozen clocks in date-sensitive tests.** `test_integration_sync_tenders` fixed its
  PNCP records at 2026-09-17 while `sync_open_tenders` built its window from
  `datetime.now()`. The two drifted apart and on **2026-09-19** the search fallback's
  stop value overtook the fixtures, so the outage test wrote zero tenders and `main`
  went red — with no production change involved (`sync_tenders.py` was byte-identical
  to B2's original commit). A test whose result depends on the day it runs is a
  failure with a delayed fuse: pin the clock next to the data it has to agree with.

| 2026-09-21 | E1 (Telegram linking + weekly digest) | in review | https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/48 | **No migration.** `telegram_links` (0001) has no column for a token's expiry, so the `/start` token carries its own: 4 bytes of expiry + 12 random, HMAC'd with a key derived from `AUTH_SECRET` under a fixed label, 42 chars — inside Telegram's 64-char `start` limit. Single use is the column (`start_token` holds `sha256(token)`, cleared in the same statement that sets `chat_id`); re-issuing supersedes. `POST /api/telegram/webhook` compares `X-Telegram-Bot-Api-Secret-Token` with `constantTimeEquals` (the same one `lib/admin/auth.ts` uses), **fails closed** on an unset or <16-char secret (503), and never calls the Bot API — it enqueues `send_telegram` keyed `reply:<update_id>` at priority 1 and calls `wakeWorker()`, so the greeting lands in ~1 s instead of up to the 2-minute idle poll. The digest is a **fan-out**: scheduler `weekly_digest` Monday 07:00 BRT → one `send_telegram` per account keyed `digest:<user_id>:<ISO week>`, so `jobs_dedupe` makes two sweeps one message and all four consumers work; tenders are chosen at send time, never in the payload. **New kill switch `TELEGRAM_DELIVERY=send`**, same word and shape as `WHATSAPP_DELIVERY` and deliberately separate — `@LicitaQuiBot` is live, so turning WhatsApp on must not turn Telegram on. §10 read from `plan_limits` (`alert` 1/week, `keywords` 1, `states` 1), week boundary in **Brasília** (a UTC one falls 21:00 Sunday in SP). §12: chat ids never logged — `telegram.chat_ref()`, the `company.cnpj_ref` shape. `/conta/alertas` built, so `ALERTS_HREF` → `ALERTS_PATH` (routes.ts) and the bell stops going to `/fundadores` (closes half of TO_VALIDATE item 7). **570 worker unit tests** (508 before), **597 web** (518 before), build green. **Post-review fix.** `TEST_DATABASE_URL_E1` was provisioned and the 17 integration tests ran for the first time; two failed, with one cause. `digests_sent_this_week` counted a dry run (deliberately, so a kill-switched machine does not loop) while `record_deliveries` fired only on a real send, so with `TELEGRAM_DELIVERY` unset — the default, and the state of production — every digest spent the week's quota and wrote nothing to `alert_deliveries`, and next Monday re-offered the same three tenders forever. `COMPLETED_EVENTS` now names the pair once and `Delivery.completed` asks the same question of an outcome; a failure still records neither. **17/17 integration, 575 unit.** **Follow-ups for Sci:** (1) the webhook is still unregistered — `setWebhook` is a production change, and a Vercel preview URL rotates so it can only point at production; (2) `plan_limits` has an `alert` row for `basico` only, so the code reads a missing cap as *uncapped* rather than zero (else founders on `promocional` get nothing on opening week) — wants rows in a migration; (3) `TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID` in `.env.local` is **the bot's own id** (8619343039), not a chat — nothing reads it, and Sci is replacing it; the 403 it produces is now a named reason code. Telegram delivery itself **is** proven (Sci pressed Start; a real message reached chat 958776226, `message_id=6`); (4) `u1.db.test.ts` and `signup.db.test.ts` race on the global 48 `founders_list.seat`s when run in parallel (seen once: `Key (seat)=(22) already exists`, green on re-run) |

"costuma vencer" → "a faixa em que os vencedores fecharam" — **3 strings, not the 2 the ruling named**: my sweep listed `radar.landing.opportunity.body` under rule 1 and failed to flag it, so the ruling was made on an incomplete list. The token was minted and the browser handed off to the bot, but `telegram_links.chat_id` stayed **null**, no `telegram_linked` event was written and no reply job was queued — the `/start` never reached the webhook, so there was no message to miss. **Suspected cause, unconfirmed:** `t.me/<bot>?start=<token>` auto-sends the payload only for a first-ever conversation; a chat with history just opens. Sci's Telegram already had a chat with @LicitaQuiBot from yesterday's delivery test, which is exactly the case that fails — and during founders week anyone who ever opened the bot hits it. Two separate problems: a functional one (the flow assumes the tap always sends `/start`) and a UX one (bounced to another app, no waiting state, no failure path, and the only confirmation is *in* Telegram, which is the channel that failed). Confirm the cause by sending `/start <token>` by hand before building |

| 2026-09-22 | E3 (account → Telegram linking journey) | in review | https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/52 | **No migration.** **The card's hypothesis does not hold, and the launch blocker is a production config mismatch only Sci can fix.** Production runtime logs show the `/start` *did* reach `POST /api/telegram/webhook` and was rejected: an unbroken stream of ~72 POSTs from **07:42 UTC onward**, spaced 60–110 s and lengthening — Telegram redelivering one un-acknowledged update, exactly as `route.ts` documents — every one answered **401 `bad_secret`**, plus **11 × 503 `not_configured`** earlier from a window with no `TELEGRAM_WEBHOOK_SECRET` set. So the `secret_token` given to `setWebhook` ≠ production's `TELEGRAM_WEBHOOK_SECRET`. **Sci: run `getWebhookInfo` (expect `last_error_message: "Wrong response from the webhook: 401 Unauthorized"`), then re-run `setWebhook` with the exact production value.** Until that is done no amount of UI fixes the link. The deep-link "returning chat swallows the payload" theory is **unproven** and nothing here special-cases it; the manual fallback covers that case and every other, without asserting a cause. E1's server path is sound and its 17 integration tests already prove it. **What shipped:** (1) `lib/telegram/handoff.ts` — a four-state hand-off (`idle`/`waiting`/`failed`/`linked`) from two facts that already exist: `telegram_links.start_token is not null and chat_id is null` (now `readLinkStatus().pending`) plus an httpOnly 15-min cookie holding the plaintext token, since the column keeps only `sha256(token)` and the manual fallback must print the real thing. The token's own signed expiry is the only clock, so no `issued_at` column. `connectTelegram` now redirects to `/conta/alertas` instead of `t.me`, which renders the deep link, the `/start <token>` to paste, a re-check and the validity window — one extra tap, bought with a hand-off that can be seen and recovered. (2) **CNPJ detour closed:** `/conta/alertas` asks for the CNPJ inline instead of sending people to the Radar. (3) **CNPJ no longer permanent:** `setUserCnpj` + `saveCompany` are the account setting `rememberUserCnpj`'s comment had been deferring to since U1; `rememberUserCnpj` is unchanged and still only fills a `null`. **Gotcha worth knowing:** `users.cnpj` is `references companies(cnpj)`, which is the real reason `rememberUserCnpj` carries its `exists` guard — so `ensureCompanyRow` inserts a key-only placeholder stamped `'epoch'` (dated *now* it would read as **fresh** and never be refilled) and `companyOrLookup` runs **first**, where an absent company enqueues at priority 1 and wakes the worker. **634 web tests** (601 on main), lint/typecheck/build green; 390 px and 1280 px checked in Chrome, no horizontal overflow. **Follow-ups for Sci:** (1) the secret mismatch above — the actual blocker; (2) a denied webhook is invisible to operators (a `console.warn` nobody watches) — worth an `/admin` health row or an alarm, O2's territory; (3) `saveCompany` writes **no** event, so a CNPJ attached from `/conta` does not count toward §14's "≥ 300 CNPJs" gate — `cnpj_searched` would be the wrong name for it, so the gate's definition is Sci's call; (4) `u1.db.test.ts`/`signup.db.test.ts` still race on the 48 `founders_list` seats (known, not mine) |

Tailwind v4 drops an unknown utility silently, so nothing failed: the background looked right only by inheriting the body's ivory, and the `h1` on `/termos` and `/privacidade` rendered at the browser default on the two pages the law requires us to publish. | 2026-09-22 | Pre-launch fixes from two production reviews (items 1–10) | in review | https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/55 | **No migration, display layer only.** Ten findings observed on the live site, verified against CNPJ `36955612000185` on production data. **(1) The headline slot.** On the first page of `compatible`, **20 of 20** cards carry no `estimated_value` — the 22px Archivo anchor read "Valor não informado" twenty times. `lib/radar/headline.ts` elects the anchor: value when there is one, else **the deadline** (the only fact that decides whether to act today, the one PNCP never withholds, and the product's own promise is "antes do prazo"), else item count, else ME/EPP regime, else nothing — the absence drops to a quiet `text-meta text-muted` line and is never the anchor. The top-right countdown steps aside when the deadline is promoted, so no card says "13 dias" twice. Same rule on `opportunity-view`, where the 28px slot collapses and the deadline block above it is already the biggest thing. **(2) Titles.** `cleanTitle()` strips the `[portal]` prefix, the trailing full stop and the shouting. Judged **per word, not per string**: agencies shout the object then paste the legal basis in prose, so one lowercase letter 300 chars in used to leave the visible 120 untouched — caught in the browser, not by a test. Acronyms and codes survive; a 4-letter acronym outside the allow-list does not, which is the accepted cost. **(3) `support.email` was `TODO(Sci)` and shipped** into `legal.dataRequest`, the LGPD channel brief §1 says must never bounce — the line was suppressed rather than wrong, which is worse. Now `contato@` for support, **`privacidade@` for data-subject requests**, and `/conta` renders the sentence again. **(4) AI notice** (§2.2 rule 5) added to `opportunity-view` (a compatibility reading) and `price-view` (money) — it was in marketing and the screening only. **(5) `--color-attention` measured 4.419:1** on its own soft fill, not the 4.9 the comment claimed against ivory: the badge is never on ivory. `#8f5200` → **5.30:1** measured, **5.299:1 confirmed in Chrome** on the 11px `VERIFICAR` badge at 320/390/1280. `success` and `muted` re-measured at 4.64:1 and left alone. New `styles/contrast.test.ts` pins all 15 ink/fill pairs the product paints text in. **(6) Seat meter:** 48 empty boxes under "Restam 48 vagas" is a picture of an empty room, and because the page is static it was **every** first paint regardless of sales. Grid now renders only above zero; verified live at 0 (no grid) and at 7 (grid returns, "Restam 41 vagas"). **(7) Bucket hints** moved into `/radar` under the tabs — they only existed on the marketing landing, which an e-mail link skips. **(8) Wordmark outlined as SVG** (2,437 B, HarfBuzz-shaped at wght 800/wdth 85, viewBox reproducing the old CSS line box exactly), so `fonts.ts` drops `axes: ['wdth']` and pins 600/700/800: **Archivo Latin 90,096 → 34,940 B (−61.2%)**, preload per route **150,448 → 95,292 B (−55,156 B, −36.7%)**, all font files −31.4%. Spec §11 updated — it required the axis. **(9) `::selection`** was the browser default lavender; now brand Blue. Graphite on that blue is **2.91:1 (fails)**, so the text is Ivory at **5.78:1**; the graphite panel gets `--color-blue-light` (text 5.19:1, fill-vs-panel 5.19:1) — both confirmed in Chrome. **(10) Density:** public section rhythm 48/64 → 40/48, **5909 → 5621 px (−288 px, −4.9%)** at 1280. **The premise did not survive measurement and the rest is Sci's call:** the signup card (1060 px) sets the hero row height, not the left column (469 px), so tightening the left column reclaims nothing above the fold and *widens* the 591 px void beside the form — capping `--text-hero` to 52px would buy a 4-line headline and make that imbalance worse. Above-the-fold element count is unchanged at 16; the hero's balance is a layout/content decision, not a spacing one. **53 test files / 705 tests** (50/638 on main), lint, typecheck and build green; 390/1280 checked and 320 for the badges, no horizontal overflow at any width, touch targets, body line-height and the 20px gutter untouched. **Follow-ups for Sci:** (a) spec §11 now says the wordmark ships as outlines — one line to revert if that is not wanted; (b) the hero balance above; (c) `u1.db.test.ts`/`signup.db.test.ts` still race on the 48 seats (known, not mine) |
| 2026-09-22 | PNCP 404 on `/itens` and `/arquivos`: stuck collector jobs | in review | https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/54 | Reported as "two jobs retrying forever" on `03788239000166-1-000229/2026`; **the evidence says something else and something worse.** The jobs (2286, 2287) were not looping — they were `failed` after 4 attempts, which is terminal and silent, and their last error was `CircuitOpen`, not the 404. The 404 itself is real and stable, and PNCP's Consulta detail endpoint says what it means: **`410 GONE — "A contratação informada foi excluída e não pode ser consultada."`** for all three affected tenders (also `05733936000145-1-000005/2026`, `46634044000174-1-000220/2026`). So the 404 was neither "no items" nor a wrong path: the agency **withdrew** the contratação after we ingested it. **The 404 did count toward the breaker**, but not the way it looks: `_get` called `record_success()` and then raised *inside* `breaker.guard()`, whose `except` recorded a failure anyway — invisible among 4xx alone (the success reset the counter first, so it never reached two, and the existing test passed throughout), fatal when mixed: one 404 left `pncp-itens` one failure short of open, so the next genuine timeout opened it and stopped **every** tender's items for 15 minutes. Now only a transport failure or a 5xx reaches the breaker. Second half of the bug: `sync_items` had **no "synced, empty" state** — `tender_items` has a row-level `updated_at` and zero rows to carry it, so a tender with no items read as stale on every sweep forever; it now writes the same `events` marker B4 writes for the file list. Verified in production: both jobs re-run **`done`**, markers `absent: withdrawn`. **Two follow-ups for Sci.** (1) **A withdrawn tender is still on the Radar, and completing its job made it *more* visible** — `roll_up` now runs and classified `03788239000166…` as `Alimentos` from its object text. Whether a withdrawn tender is hidden, greyed out or left alone is a product decision the spec does not cover (§3.2 has no row for it); nothing here changes what users see beyond that classification. (2) **Proposed schema PR, not shipped here** (CLAUDE.md: migrations are their own PR): `tenders.items_synced_at`/`files_synced_at` + `withdrawn_at` columns to replace the two `events` markers, and a `parked` value on `jobs_status_check` so a regression can be parked for review instead of ending as an ordinary `failed`. Three older jobs (4108, 7395, 7396) are stuck the same way and will never self-heal, since their tenders are excluded and can no longer change — left alone as out of this card's scope; re-runnable once this is deployed |

The zero-stripping (`000958` → `958`) is not inferred: PNCP writes the same triple into the file URLs we store verbatim in `tender_files.url`. Verified live in Chrome on three production tenders, one per modality — Pregão - Eletrônico `87612826000190-1-000958/2026`, Dispensa `01298975000100-1-000157/2026`, Concorrência - Eletrônica `18663401000197-1-000233/2026` — each portal page printing our id back character for character. The **whole Objeto** now renders verbatim above the screening CTA, no toggle (§2.2 rule 4: the AI is a reading *of this text*, so the reader gets the source first), `whitespace-pre-line` to keep the órgãos' own lotes list. 54 files / 745 tests, +1 file and +40 tests over `main` at #55. **Duplicate editais: reported, not fixed** — 92 near-duplicate groups, 94 redundant listings, 2.0% of the Radar, across 77 agencies, by the rule *same CNPJ + year + item count + item sum + normalised-object similarity ≥ 0.90*; `…-000958/2026` and `…-000963/2026` are both on PNCP as "Edital nº 67/2026" and "Edital nº 0067/2026". Proposal in the PR is to **group, not delete**. | 2026-09-22 | Radar **list** UX: the three failures Sci hit in one sitting | in review | https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/57 (merged) + https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/62 | All three reproduced on production with CNPJ `36955612000185` before a line changed, and measured again after. **(1) Going back threw the search away.** The query was always in the URL; the *screen* was not: `RadarScreen` re-ran its whole pipeline on every mount, so Back re-analysed the CNPJ and dropped every "Ver mais" page — 60 cards and 3000 px, back to a spinner. `lib/radar/list-cache.ts` restores the list before the first paint: every loaded page, the cursor, the tab and the scroll. **Staleness rule, from the spec and not invented:** under 60 s restored with **no request at all** (the sweep owns this data and runs every 30 min); 60 s–30 min restored and refreshed behind it (§3.1); past 30 min discarded, because §3.2 is the TTL of an open tender and `proposals_close_at > now()` is evaluated in Postgres, not in the tab. A restored snapshot keeps its **original** timestamp, so being shown again does not renew its lease. The in-app way back was separately broken: `opportunity-screen.tsx` builds "Voltar" from `cnpj`/`uf`/`q`/`group` and the cards linked to a bare `/radar/edital/…`, so it went to a bare `/radar` — `tenderHrefFrom()` carries the search. **(2) Reading the Objeto cost clicks for text we already hold:** 16 of the 20 cards on page 1 truncate at 120 chars (median 205, longest 512) and the full string is in the list payload, so "Objeto completo" is a `<details>` on the card — no request, no screening, collapsed by default, and **no control at all** on the 4 cards whose object already fits. `<details>` cannot live inside an `<a>`, so the card is now a surface holding a link and a disclosure (two keyboard stops on cards that have more to show). **(3) An empty tab:** `readGroup()` answered `'compatible'` for an absent group unconditionally, so the real query `q=pavimentação asfáltica` (`{0, 0, 39}`) opened on an empty Compatíveis. Absent now means *unchosen* and `bestGroup()` elects a populated tab from the counts the route already returns; a tab the user pressed is never second-guessed (`radarHref()` writes `group=compatible` out so the two URLs differ); the chip counts are announced with their noun instead of as a bare digit; an empty tab says "Ver 39 editais em Palavras" and links there; "every tab is empty" is its own sentence (`radar.list.allEmpty.*`). **Verified in Chrome at 390 px and 1280 px against production data** — headless over CDP for the scripted runs, a visible window for the screenshots: both ways back (browser Back, and "Voltar" as a fresh document) restore 60 cards, "Mostrando 60 de 150", scroll 3000, no analyzing card, **zero requests**; the unchosen search lands on Palavras in 3 requests and on 0 when restored; no horizontal scroll at 390. **57 test files / 802 tests**, lint, typecheck and build green (main alone: 57/792). **Follow-ups for Sci:** `radar.states.emptyBody` still says "Amplie as UFs ou adicione uma palavra-chave" on a search that already has a keyword — the action line under it now carries the useful instruction, but the sentence could be better; and the background refresh re-reads only page 1, so rows further down keep the values they were loaded with (the "atualizado há X" line above states their age). **Assumption recorded, not taken as decided:** the two thresholds (60 s, 30 min) are named constants taken from §3.1/§3.2 rather than a product decision |

Legacy 2-field cursors still decode. **`status` moved onto `TenderCard`**, so the type system — not memory — makes every construction site decide; that is what caught `opportunity-view.test.tsx` carrying `'Recebendo propostas'`, a value the domain table does not have. **Contrast:** banner and chip reuse `--color-attention` on `--color-attention-soft` at **5.30:1** (already pinned); the first draft quieted the source line with `opacity-90`, which blends it to **4.37:1** and fails AA — removed, and the pairing added to `contrast.test.ts` with that note. **Fixture:** the HUPE-RJ tender itself captured from production into `lib/radar/hupe.fixture.ts` with `situacao_id` 4, serving B9's rendering now and B10's diff later. **59 files / 829 tests** (57/806 at `696d3d0`), lint/typecheck/build green. Verified in Chrome at 390 px and 1280 px on production data — suspended `46854998000192-1-000556/2026` (PMSP, closes 25/09, still open) shows the banner, the SUSPENSA chip, "Prazo suspenso"/"data anterior" and zero urgency; Divulgada control `03061303000102-1-000124/2026` still reads "2 dias / restantes" and "Ainda dá tempo" and is pixel-unchanged; no horizontal overflow at either width. **Follow-ups for Sci:** (1) **the Portuguese copy needs your ruling (brief §5)** — 15 new strings under `radar.status` plus `radar.card.previousDeadline`, listed in the PR; the load-bearing one is "Suspensão não é cancelamento: o órgão pode retomar este edital com novas datas", which exists because §3.1 forbids wording a suspension as final; (2) the Radar **list** was verified through the API, not the rendered page — React does not hydrate in this Chrome profile (an extension injects `bis_skin_checked`, visible as a hydration mismatch in the dev log), so the card's rendering is covered by tests on the real payload instead; (3) `u1.db.test.ts`/`signup.db.test.ts` still race on the 48 `founders_list` seats (known, not mine) |
| 2026-09-22 | Neon out of disk: writes failing in production | done | https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/PENDING | **Outage, not a warning.** `psycopg.errors.DiskFull: project size limit (512 MB) has been exceeded` while backfilling `short_title`; every insert, update and delete was failing. Project was at **496.7 MB**: `neondb` 293 MB (of which `tender_items` **231 MB** — 127,980 rows at ~1.6 KB of heap each against a **175-character** average description, i.e. upsert bloat, not text) and 16 `licitaqui_test_*` databases at 195.9 MB. Truncated every data table in all 16 test databases, keeping `schema_migrations`, `plan_limits` and `cnae_segments` — **freed 53.5 MB**, project now 445.5 MB. The number that matters is what it did **not** free: an **empty** test database still costs **8.8 MB**, so 16 of them are **141 MB of irreducible overhead — 27% of the free tier** whether a test runs or not; only ~55 MB was real data. Growth is ~35 MB/day (≈1,000 tenders/day x 22 items), so the headroom is under two days. `VACUUM FULL` on a 207 MB heap needs roughly double that free and may reclaim nothing on copy-on-write storage, so production was left untouched. Spec **§14.1** (vendored here) records the rules: CI and production never share a storage budget; check the budget before creating a database or running a backfill; `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE ... WHERE t IS DISTINCT FROM excluded`; `tender_items` gets a retention policy; prefer lazy item fetching; a backfill must be resumable, must never swallow a `DiskFull` as success, and must measure its own storage delta. Follow-ups carded by Sci: no-op upserts, retention, lazy fetching, **CI in its own Neon project**, and an `/admin` storage alarm at 80% with a Telegram ping |
| 2026-09-22 | Tender detail: one tab strip, and the items on it | in review | https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/64 | Two screens of the same tender had two design systems. **After** the triagem it has tabs (`Resumo · Documentos · Exigências`); **before** it, the detail screen ended in a loose `EDITAL E ANEXOS · CRIAR CONTA` button with a bare `.zip` link hanging under the CTA — Sci: *"I believe we need to keep the same design system and have the file in the same tabs, not placed surfing anywhere."* And the thing he opens PNCP for was missing: *"we have it in the TAB ITENS… without it, I have to open two applications."* **Reused, not rewritten:** canvas 04's strip moved out of `screening-view.tsx` into `components/tabs.tsx`; both screens import it, and it grew `aria-controls`/`aria-labelledby` on the way. **Tab set: `Itens · Documentos`, Itens first** — two and not PNCP's five because we hold two tables; Itens first because it is PNCP's own first tab and the one that is never locked. Header, deadline/value card, **Objeto**, why-list and Operação all stay **outside** the tabs. **Items were already on the wire** — `GET /api/tenders/:id` has always returned `items` from our own `tender_items`; nothing new is fetched and PNCP is never called in a request (§3). **Columns** in PNCP's vocabulary: Número · Descrição · Quantidade · Valor unitário estimado · Valor total estimado, with `moneyExact()` (two decimals, never scaled — `money()` would print `R$ 2,99 mi`, which cannot be checked against anything). Verified live on `12342663000173-1-000017/2026`: 15 rows, item 1 `400 Hora × R$ 816,67 = R$ 326.668,00`, sum **R$ 2.988.571,02** — PNCP's own header figure. **Paging, measured:** production runs far past the card's "55+" — the largest tender holds **1 124 items**, 122 tenders exceed 200. 20 rows a page (`Ver mais itens` / `Mostrando 20 de 1124 itens`); in Chrome the 1 124-item tender renders **657 DOM nodes** instead of ~29 000, the whole list being >20× the markup. Virtualisation rejected on this data: row heights run from one line to an expanded 2 048-character spec, it would need browser measurement (so no SSR and no `renderToStaticMarkup` tests) and it breaks find-in-page. **390 px:** cards below `md`, a real `<table>` with `<th scope="col">` from `md` up — one of the two is always `display:none`, so exactly one is in the a11y tree and neither fakes table semantics with ARIA. Five columns cannot be read in a 390 px frame; an `overflow-x` table would make the reader scroll away from the description to reach the price. **Descriptions** over 160 chars collapse behind the same `<details>` the Radar card used, **verbatim — not de-shouted**: the column exists to be checked against PNCP's cell, and case carries meaning in a specification. **The total is never a substitute for the tender's value** — `estimated_value` is null on **5 036 of 5 921** tenders with items today and on **42** it already differs from the sum of the agency's own rows, so the figure is labelled `Soma dos N itens deste edital` with its arithmetic named (§2.2 rule 3) and omitted entirely if any row lacks a total. **Documentos:** the §8 account gate is unchanged, but the lock is now a state of the tab (a `LockedBlock` a visitor can open and read) instead of an absence outside it; `files: []` gets its own sentence. **Radar card:** `Objeto completo` removed (Sci: *"we will have the whole description inside the item"*), so the card is a single `CardLink` again — one keyboard stop per tender, the regression the `<details>` had forced. `lib/radar/object.ts` existed only for that disclosure and went with it. **B9 survives:** suspended `10735145000194-1-000138/2026` (827 items) still banners *"Edital SUSPENSO pelo órgão em 21/09/2026"* above everything and shows **zero** urgency strings, tabs and all. **Contrast, measured in Chrome on the rendered pairs:** sum label `#6b6b67` on `#f3eee8` **4.64:1**, sum figure `#171717` on `#f3eee8` **15.54:1** (pair added to `contrast.test.ts`), card figure label on surface **5.35:1**, `Ver descrição completa` `#2457d6` on `#ffffff` **6.16:1**, active tab on ivory **5.78:1**, inactive tab / column headers / unit / row number **5.02:1**. No `opacity` anywhere. **59 files / 885 tests** (59/830 at `efeaf77`), lint · typecheck · build green. Verified in real Chrome at **390 px and 1280 px** on production data: no horizontal overflow at either width on the 15-, 827- and 1 124-item tenders. **Also fixes a silent regression this PR surfaced:** PR #56 moved the **Objeto** to directly under the deadline/value box, spanning the content column at `max-w-[68ch]`, because Sci asked for it by name — *"The Objeto should be right after the box with the price… The Objeto will place the entire weight below to the box."* `task/b9-tender-status` branched **before** #56 landed and its merge `cc4b766` resolved the conflict in its own favour, putting the block back under the two-column grid, restoring `max-w-[62ch]` **and deleting the three tests that guarded the order** (ten `it()` blocks under `OpportunityView · the whole Objeto` on #56, seven on `main`). B9's own diff touches `FullObject` **zero times**, so none of it was decided — and with the guards gone CI stayed green while undoing a change Sci had asked for. Restored here: the placement, the 68 ch measure, #56's reasoning comment and all three tests, **plus a fourth** pinning the Objeto above `role="tablist"`. Verified the way #56's lane did — re-applying B9's revert fails exactly those three, and pushing the Objeto below the tabs fails the fourth. Confirmed in Chrome at both widths: the chain reads box → Objeto → Por que → Operação → tabs, and at 1280 px the Objeto is a **sibling** of the grid spanning all 920 px with the measure capped at 571 px. **Follow-ups for Sci:** (1) **20 new Portuguese strings need your ruling (brief §5)** — `radar.opportunity.tabs.*`, `radar.opportunity.items.*`, `radar.opportunity.files.*`, listed in the PR; the load-bearing one is `items.sumNote`, which is what stops the sum reading as the agency's declared total; (2) **`tenders.short_title`**: once the AI title lands the card headline is a paraphrase and the list stops carrying the real subject anywhere — my recommendation is **not** to bring `Objeto completo` back but to print the real objeto as a muted second line under the generated title, because the defect then is a missing *fact*, not a missing *tail*; (3) the Radar **list** page could not be exercised live — the CNPJ lookup waits on the Python worker, which the parallel `worker/` lane is occupying — so the card is covered by tests plus the Landing's real-data example panel, which renders the changed component; (4) the screening screen's strip is covered by its own tests rather than in the browser: running a triagem calls OpenRouter |
| 2026-09-22 | Tender `estimated_value`: "Valor não informado" on 85% of the Radar | in review | https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/65 | **No migration — `tenders.next_refresh_at` was already there, defined and indexed by §6.1 and read by nothing.** The cause is ADR-0001's search fallback and the correlation is exact: **5,080 of 5,965 rows carry a search-index payload and 0 of them have a value; the 885 consulta-sourced rows all do.** The index publishes no `valorTotalEstimado`, no `srp` and no `orcamentoSigilosoCodigo`, and B2 enqueues `sync_items`/`sync_files` for a changed tender but **never a header re-read** — so a row that entered degraded stayed degraded for life. The 885 are frozen from the last healthy stretch while the search-sourced set grows every cycle, so coverage **decays on its own**: Sci measured 20% of 4,430, I measured 14.8% of 5,965, and 12.7% of 6,133 four hours later. The ADR's asymmetry argument does not cover this — a re-sweep only rewrites a row whose `pncp_updated_at` moved, so the *window* heals and the *row* does not. **Two sources, one order:** the Consulta detail's `valorTotalEstimado` wins whenever it answers (Sci's rule is that we show what the portal shows); otherwise the sum of `tender_items.total_value`, which `items.roll_up` already computed to decide `favored_treatment` and threw away. Precedence lives once in `items.pick_total`, shared with `favored_treatment`, so a card's value and the ME/EPP claim beside it cannot come from different arithmetic. **The agreement rate is 94.7%, not 100%** — measured across the 777 production tenders holding both a positive header and items summing above zero: 736 agree to the cent, 41 do not (19 within 1%, 17 at 1–2×, 5 at ≥2×, largest 13.3×). The item sum is never *lower*; where it differs it **over-counts**, because grouped lots and ME/EPP cotas are listed beside the items they are carved from (one has 283 item rows and 71 distinct descriptions). Consulta therefore always wins, and every fallback row records `value_source: items` in a `tender_value:<id>` marker so the minority stays findable and the rate stays queryable. **It writes columns, not `raw`** — the obvious `from_consulta`/upsert implementation merges the detail payload in, which `pg_column_size` puts at **+2,822 B × 5,373 rows ≈ 14 MB against 15.3 MB of headroom**; it would have filled the disk rather than fixed the Radar. **Three ways in, because each has a hole the next covers:** the fallback enqueues `refresh_tender_value` for every row it writes (root cause, but it fires exactly when consulta is down); `sweep_tender_values` runs every 30 min for whatever is still unvalued; `next_refresh_at` backs a tender off (6 h header TTL per §3.2, 30 days after a `410`) so the sweep cannot spin. **410/301:** a `410 GONE` parks the contratação rather than retiring it — what a withdrawn tender looks like on the Radar stays Sci's call, as `absence.py` already records — and neither the 410 nor a 404 touches the `pncp-consulta` breaker. The retired `/api/pncp/v1/orgaos/…` spelling answers **301 with no `Location` header at all**, so `follow_redirects` is powerless and httpx hands the 301 back; `_http_error` now says so instead of leaving a bare "HTTP 301" to re-measure, and the path constant is pinned by a test. **Latent bug fixed on the way:** `confidential_budget` was the one column `UPSERT_SQL` did **not** COALESCE, so a fallback sweep passing over a consulta-sourced tender overwrote a real `true` with the dataclass's `False` default; it is now `None` ("unknown") on search-sourced rows, and `apps/web` already coerces with `Boolean(...)` so a NULL renders exactly as the old `false` — no web change needed or made. **Verified against live PNCP + production, read-only:** on Sci's two worked tenders the portal figure, the consulta header and our item sum agree to the cent — `87612826000190-1-000958/2026` → R$ 271.350,31 and `12342663000173-1-000017/2026` → R$ 2.988.571,02 — and both carry `srp=true`, which explains the null `price_registration` beside a "Sistema de Registro de Preços" object. **52 new tests** (24 unit + 28 integration), full worker suite and `ruff` green. **Follow-ups for Sci.** (1) **The backfill has NOT been run** — the Neon project is out of disk (496.7 of 512 MB; the coordinator hit `DiskFull` mid-write on the short-title backfill) and freeing space is Sci's call because it involves the test databases CI depends on. No production writes were made by this branch; every number above is a `SELECT`. `scripts/backfill_tender_values.py` is ready, was exercised with `--dry-run` (5,356 due, 18 of a 20-row sample valuable from items alone, before/after identical), is **resumable by construction** (its work queue is a query, not a cursor), counts a `DiskFull` as `failed` and never as upgraded, and leaves `next_refresh_at` alone so a failed row is still due next run. Expected coverage once run: **777 → ~5,200 of 6,133 (12.7% → ~85%)**, ~8 MB of MVCC churn that autovacuum reclaims and **zero** permanent growth. (2) **The real storage lever is not this table:** `tender_items` is **231 MB of the 293 MB** database and **155 MB of that is the per-item `raw` jsonb** — 1,268 B/row against 175 characters of description (21 MB of descriptions in total) — i.e. every field `items.from_pncp` extracts is stored twice. Trimming it is a schema-and-backfill decision, deliberately not slipped into this lane. (3) **Its headroom check sums all 18 databases on the branch** (458 MB), because `pg_database_size(current_database())` answers 305 MB and would have reported 207 MB free on a project that had just hit `DiskFull` — worth knowing for any other backfill. (4) **For ADR-0001's outstanding re-measurement:** `/api/consulta` was **flapping, not down**, throughout this work — 6×200 (~0.8 s), 1×502, 1×503, 6×read-timeout over 14 minute-spaced probes (~43% success) while `/api/search/` and `/api/pncp/v1/…/itens` answered everything under 2 s. That is a third failure mode the ADR has not characterised, and the one that makes a per-tender backfill slow rather than impossible; recorded in the ADR along with the consequence section |

The cap's argument was real (a long measure of dense uppercase legal prose is hard to read) and it lost to alignment, which is Sci's call. The `max-w-` is gone; all three blocks now measure **920px at 1280 / 350px at 390**. The test that pinned the cap was **inverted, not deleted** — `spans the full column: any width cap here is a regression` now fails on `max-w-[68ch]`, on `max-w-[62ch]` and on `max-w-prose`, each verified by mutation; #56's reasoning comment was **kept and extended** to record that the cap existed, why, and that Sci overruled it on 2026-09-22, because the next person to notice the long line will be right about the typography and still wrong about the screen. **The measure was compensated with leading, never width** (the one lever left): measured in Chrome on the worst object we hold — `82558909000124-1-000279/2026`, 1 928 characters, no line breaks of its own — the uncapped block renders at **140–145 characters per line** at 1280px, not the ~105 the old comment estimated, and only **294 of 6 299** objects carry the agency's own newlines, so that unbroken flow is the normal case. `leading-relaxed` → `leading-loose` (1.625 → 2.0): ~73px of height on that worst case, ~10px on a typical two-line object, and at 390px the same text is 51–57 characters per line where the leading is simply comfortable. **2 · Separation between the topics below it.** Sci: *"for the two sections below, we need a little separation, increase the padding between each topic."* Numbers taken from `app/(public)/sections.tsx`, which already solves this exact shape (a two-column block that stacks) on the rhythm PR #55 settled at 40/48 — no new scale for the app screens: between topics `pt-7` + `main`'s `gap-3.5` = **42px** (≈ #55's 40), stacked below 900px `gap-7` = **28px**, columns at ≥900px `min-[900px]:gap-10` = **40px** (was 32). `pt-7` on the grid **and** on `<Record>`, so the pair is not left floating with 42px above and 14px below; the header's tightly coupled rows (title → badges → card) stay at 14px because they are one topic. Measured live at both widths: 42 / 28-or-40 / 42. **Order and B9 unchanged:** all four guards pass, the chain still reads box → Objeto → Por que → Operação → tabs, and suspended `02858064000153-1-000012/2026` still banners above everything with `urgency: []` at 390 and 1280. No horizontal overflow at either width. **No new Portuguese strings** — this is layout only; the 20 strings from #64 are still waiting on Sci. **59 files / 889 tests**, unchanged in count because nothing here added logic; lint · typecheck · build green. `git diff origin/main...HEAD --stat` lists exactly two files, both deliberate |
| 2026-09-22 | Radar navigation: the search dies at the triagem link, and the screening result never appears | in review | https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/68 | Two defects Sci hit on production, both on canvas 04. **1 · The search died at the triagem link.** PR #57 fixed the card → tender hop by adding `tenderHrefFrom` *next to* the bare `tenderHref`, which left the bare form as the shortest thing to type — so the same bug was one screen along, on the CTA at `opportunity-view.tsx:728` (`${tenderHref(id)}/triagem`), and on eight more: `priceHref`, the locked price block, both `accountHref` links, the price item chips, `TenderCardView`'s bare **default** href, and the two Suspense fallbacks in `page.tsx`. Confirmed on production before the fix — the card carried `?cnpj&group`, the triagem link carried nothing, and two presses of Voltar landed on a bare `/radar`. **The call: there is no bare form any more.** No link out of a Radar screen wants one, so keeping a "deliberately named" bare export would recreate the exact shape that produced this twice. `tenderHref`/`screeningHref`/`priceHref`/`radarHref` take a **required** `RadarSearch` — that is what found all nine call sites — and `readSearch()` reads the four travelling parameters once per screen. `RadarSearch` is deliberately the same four fields `listKey` uses, so #62's snapshot restore is cooperated with rather than bypassed; a test round-trips the triagem URL back through `readSearch`→`radarHref` to the Radar address it came from. Types cannot catch the mistake that actually shipped (a URL built by hand from a path and a literal, which is a *string*, not a call), so `client.test.ts` reads every file under `app/radar/**` with comments stripped and fails on one — mutation-checked by reintroducing the original line. **2 · The screening result never appeared.** Reproduced on production, CNPJ 36955612000185 on `13654405000195-1-000033/2026`: "está demorando" at 60 s with **no further request**, while a probe on `GET …/screening` got `ready`/`ok` at **≈178 s**. Cause: §3.1's 60 s is the budget for a one-hop read, and an `ai_screening` is `sync_files` + a 120 s download + `extract_text` + a 90 s model call (§7.1, §7.2) — the poll was structurally certain to give up first, and it gave up by *stopping*. **Quota was never burned twice**: the second visit's `POST` answered **200** and the badge stayed `1 de 2`, because `quota.spend` de-duplicates on the tender id — so the severity is UX, not billing. Fix: `waitThenWatch` in `poll.ts` keeps §3.1's twenty ticks, then tells the screen (honest card stays) and **keeps reading** every 10 s for 5 min, so the answer replaces the card where the user is standing; a `visibilitychange`/`pageshow` re-read covers a phone backgrounded for longer. Nothing is persisted — the trap that a Radar link is a plain `<a>` is why: the answer is a row in `ai_analyses` and the `POST` re-reads it free on the way back. **59 files / 907 tests** (main: 889), lint, typecheck, build green. `apps/web/**` only. **Needs Sci:** `radar.screening.timeoutBody` reworded — it used to say "Atualize em alguns instantes", which is now wrong; drafted as *"…aparece nesta tela assim que terminar. Não precisa sair nem pedir de novo."* (brief §5, approve or reword). **Assumptions:** `WATCH_TIMEOUT_MS` = 5 min is derived from §7.2's budgets, not from a measured tail (the one screening timed here took 178 s); `readSearch` now reads an absent `?group=` as *unchosen* rather than coercing it to `compatible`, because `group.ts` makes those different addresses and `restoreList` already bridges them. **Browser verification is incomplete and is the one thing outstanding:** the Chrome extension has no site permission for the preview host `licitaqui-1esemg772-scintechns-projects.vercel.app`, and a local dev server cannot be pointed at the database because `.env*` is deny-listed for agents. The pre-fix behaviour of *both* defects is confirmed on production at 1280 px; the post-fix walk-through at 390/1280 px needs either that host allowed in the extension or a run by Sci |
| 2026-09-22 | Radar: the CTA does not know a screening already exists (defect 3, same PR as the two above) | in review | https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/68 | Sci: *"I already have the AI Triage for this item … but the button remains like the first time, for my user."* Same missing state as defect 2 — that one is "the result does not appear when it finishes", this is "the product forgets it ever finished". **The quota question, settled first because it decides the rest: re-opening your own triagem is FREE, and the expensive reading of this defect does not exist.** `quota.spend` de-duplicates on `reference` (the tender id), so a second request by the same spender returns `repeat`/`charged: false`. Verified twice on production — a reload of an already-read triagem answered `POST` **200** with the badge unmoved at `1 de 2`, and Sci's own tender `94703980000132-1-000080/2026` opened instantly at `2 de 2` without decrementing. Pinned in `screening.db.test.ts` against a real migrated database: a cached reading charges **once** and never again for the same tender. **What *is* charged, by design:** a caller who has never opened this tender pays even when the analysis already exists, because `requestScreening` spends *before* it reads the cache — §8's own words ("returns it and records usage") and §3.2/§10 together, where the analysis is shared but the *reading* is allocated per user. Not a bug; it is why `ai_analyses` has no `user_id`. **Latent, for Sci:** `spend`'s de-duplication is period-scoped, so a Básico user (5/month) who read a tender in September is charged again for the same row in October. Visitors are unaffected (`period = 'total'`). A §10 commercial call, not taken here. **The fix:** `GET /api/tenders/:id` now carries `screening: { ready, spent }`. `ready` is **not** a new notion of freshness — it is `currentAnalysis`, the predicate factored out of `readScreening` and now shared, so the button can never promise something the triagem screen will not do; a republished edital moves `files_hash`, `ready` goes false, and the CTA honestly offers to analyse again (pinned by a test that asserts the two agree). `spent` is the `usage` half. Both in **one** statement on a route that was already doing three, rather than a second request — the CTA has to be right on first paint, and a button that changes its mind under the user's finger is worse than one that is merely wrong. It costs one extra `plan_limits` read plus one combined probe; read-only, spends nothing. **59 files / 918 tests** (889 at main, 907 after the first two defects). **Needs Sci — new customer-facing copy, brief §5:** one label became two, plus a caption line under the button, because opening a reading you have paid for and spending a triagem are different actions that were sharing the words "Ver triagem por IA". Drafted: `screeningCtaNew` "Fazer triagem por IA"; `screeningCost` "Usa 1 das suas triagens."; `screeningCostReady` "Este edital já foi lido pela IA. Abrir usa 1 das suas triagens."; `screeningDone` "Você já usou uma triagem neste edital. Abrir de novo não custa nada." `screeningCta` is unchanged and now only appears when it is true. §2.2 rule 5's AI notice still sits above the CTA, untouched |
| 2026-09-23 | §14.1 item 4: stop rewriting unchanged `tender_items` | in review | https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/69 | **Two changes, and shipping the first alone is a regression.** `tender_items` is 142,127 rows rewritten on every sweep whether or not anything changed, and on Neon's copy-on-write storage every rewrite costs twice. **(1) The guard** is an explicit 14-column `IS DISTINCT FROM`, *not* the obvious whole-row form — `where tender_items is distinct from excluded` is a **silent no-op**, because `updated_at = now()` sits in the SET list so `excluded` always differs, every row is rewritten exactly as before, and a measurement taken against it reports 0% with nothing to explain it. `GUARDED_COLUMNS` sits beside the statement and a test reconciles it against the INSERT's column list, because a column in one and not the other would be written once and then never updated again — silent per-field staleness rather than a loud failure. **(2) Freshness had to move with it.** `read_state` aged a tender *with* items on `min(tender_items.updated_at)`, which only answered "when did we last read PNCP" *because every read rewrote every row*. Guard the upsert and that coincidence breaks: `min` freezes, every unchanged tender reads `ttl` stale on every sweep for ever, and the collector re-fetches the whole corpus — worse than the bloat. The two ladders collapse to one signal, `coalesce(m.synced_at, min(i.updated_at))`. **The fallback is the cutover, and it is not optional:** measured on production, **0 of the 6,473** tenders holding items have a marker (it is new; they predate it), so reading the marker alone makes every one of them `never` — a distinct branch with **no age**, which the 12 h TTL therefore **cannot stagger**. All 6,473 would become eligible on the same sweep, against a consulta endpoint measured at **62% success in runs of two to four minutes** — the condition most likely to open a breaker and stop the collector for everyone. "Let the TTL stagger it" is the thundering herd with a reassuring name. The fallback preserves the old semantics exactly: a tender fresh before is fresh after, same age, and the staggering comes free because those timestamps are already spread. **2,736 of the 6,473 are fresh today** and would otherwise have been fetched immediately. **Self-healing per tender:** `sync_items` writes the marker unconditionally after any sync, so a marker-less tender takes the fallback exactly once — reads stale, fetches, marks, and the marker governs thereafter. Three paths return without marking (unknown tender, fresh, `DataVanished`) and none can strand a row, because **a guard on writes cannot change a path that writes nothing**. **`backfill_markers()`** dates each marker to the tender's own `min(updated_at)`, **not `now()`** — `now()` would mark the corpus fresh and defer every re-read by a TTL, the same herd moved twelve hours out; `min` not `max` because a half-finished fetch leaves some rows fresh and some old and the old ones are the truth; structurally unable to invent a marker for a zero-item tender (the join yields no group) and `not exists` so it cannot overwrite the one deliberate "synced, empty" marker. Idempotent, so safe before *or* after deploy — an optimisation, not a step the cutover depends on. **Measured, A/B on the test database** (same rows, same sweeps, control = this statement with the `WHERE` stripped): guarded **0 of 5,000** rewritten and **8 kB** growth; unguarded **5,000 of 5,000** and **912 kB** — **99.1% of the churn avoided**, control perfectly linear at 1,000 rewrites per sweep. Production scale: 142,127 rows, 232 MB heap, **1,709 B per row** is what one rewrite churns, so a full re-sync churns one entire heap (232 MB) unguarded against ~0 guarded. Skip rate is **instrumented, not asserted** — `upsert_items` returns `UpsertItemsResult(offered, written, pruned)` and it lands in the job log line and the marker per tender; a test pins psycopg's `executemany` rowcount accumulation, because that quietly becoming -1 would turn every reported rate into a fiction without failing anything. **46 tests** (25 new integration + 21 existing unit), both freshness directions proven. **Follow-ups for Sci.** (a) **Production skip-rate not yet taken** — the value backfill is still writing (5,629/6,740); it needs one full collector cycle after that to read the real rate. (b) **`raw` is the size; this PR is only the churn** — 1,268 of every 1,709 bytes per row is the per-item `raw` jsonb, about **172 MB of the 232 MB heap (74%)**, every field `from_pncp` extracts stored a second time. Trimming it reclaims ~172 MB one-off and makes every future row ~4× cheaper, far more than the guard saves, but `absence.py` and any future re-mapping read `raw`, so it is a schema-and-backfill decision and deliberately **not built here**. (c) **`files.py` is the precedent, not a second site** — `tender_files` has **no `updated_at` column at all** and `sync_files` already reads freshness from the marker, i.e. it is already the shape this PR moves `sync_items` into; it does rewrite unchanged rows but at 5.6 MB the payoff is marginal. (d) **CI**: the integration job was cancelled at 30m18s on #67 and the timeout is now a stopgap 45 min; a `--durations=0` profile shows **setup 49.0% + teardown 30.7% = 79.7% fixture overhead**, three Neon connections per DB test (two purely for cleanup DELETEs), so collapsing them in `conftest.py` targets ~60% of the runtime where a top-40 offender hunt could only reach 11.6%. Separate card, not scheduled |

| 2026-09-23 | Cards B11 and B12: breaker behaviour, from a live PNCP outage | in review | https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/PENDING | Carded by Sci, B11 first. **B11** — 59 `sync_tender_awards` jobs failed on `CircuitOpen` and each burned one of its four attempts **without a request leaving the process**; the breaker raises before the call, so `attempts` counted attempts that never happened, and a long enough outage kills a job for reasons unrelated to it (the #54 shape: transient made terminal). Fix is deferral without incrementing, with `run_after` from the breaker reset rather than the 2/8/30 ladder — the second half is not optional, since skipping only the increment leaves the backoff stuck on tier one and the job wakes every 2 min (~90 `UPDATE`s over three hours instead of ~12). Deferring the dequeue by kind was rejected: `sync_items` touches `pncp-itens` *and* `pncp-consulta` via `absence.py`, so a kind→breaker map is not clean, and refusing to dequeue blocks work that would have succeeded. **B12** — 50 minute-spaced probes of `/api/consulta` measured **62% success failing in runs of 2–4 minutes**, which is neither *down* nor *degraded*; a consecutive-failure breaker trips on a run of two, and a 15-min reset makes one bad minute cost fifteen good ones. Hence **a 62%-success service can be less available than a 40%-success one** when the first fails in runs. Evaluate a windowed policy against the recorded trace before changing production. Context: at 07:48 five of seven breakers were open or half-open, `sync_items`/`sync_files` stalled from 01:02, and the worker was healthy throughout (19 h uptime, 4/4 consumers) — the queue state was the breakers working, not a defect |
| 2026-09-23 | Radar: `R$ 0,00` where PNCP withheld the budget (found on production by Sci) | in review | https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/72 | Sci: *"Sometimes the value is blocked — 'sigiloso'. However, we hold and show it as R$ 0,00."* **Two halves; the first ships regardless of PNCP.** **(1) Zero is never a price.** PNCP publishes `valorTotalEstimado: 0.0` in the value field when a budget is withheld — asked directly, `13112669000117-1-000010/2026` returns that alongside `orcamentoSigilosoCodigo: 3` ("Compra totalmente sigilosa") — and **108 tenders hold `estimated_value = 0`** while Sci's two examples hold `NULL` with every item row zero. Printing it states a figure the edital does not support: brief **§2.2 rule 3**. Confirmed on production before the fix, on Sci's own tender at `/radar/edital/94703980000132-1-000080/2026`: **3 × "R$ 0,00"** in the rendered text; on the branch, **0**. **The guard sits in `money()`/`moneyExact()`, not at the call sites,** because the failure modes are asymmetric: a missed call site prints a false price *silently* (the bug, and the one that recurs the day a sixth value slot is added), while a formatter refusing a zero some future caller meant fails *loudly*, in front of the developer who wrote it. `format.ts` documents the boundary it costs — a billing screen where "você pagou R$ 0,00" is a fact must not reach for these two and gets its own formatter. Deliberately **not** guarded: a sub-R$1 value `money()` would round to "R$ 0" — same untruth in principle, no such row in the corpus, and the honest repair is a different format, not a null that hides a figure we hold. **Five places, not the four in the brief:** card headline slot · detail `VALOR ESTIMADO` box · items table unit column · items table total column · **`price-view.tsx`**, which had a second `Intl.NumberFormat` of its own and would have kept printing "Edital paga (estimado) R$ 0,00" after the other four were fixed; it now delegates to `moneyExact` (one behaviour change: the nbsp after `R$` becomes an ordinary space, matching the Itens tab). **`sumItems` now treats a zero total as a hole, like a null one** — a sum counting it as nothing would be adding up rows the table itself prints "—" for, under a caption promising *"a soma dos valores totais das linhas acima"*; the block used to vanish silently and now says why. **(2) Three states, decided once.** `tenderBudget()` in `headline.ts`, shared by the card and the detail screen so they cannot drift: `confidential_budget` → "Valor sigiloso"; a non-zero figure → the figure; anything else → "Valor não informado". **A zero is never promoted to "sigiloso"** — that is a claim about the órgão's decision, carried only by `orcamentoSigilosoCodigo` ∈ {2,3}; PNCP publishes zero for merely incomplete tenders as readily as for secret ones. Keeping them apart is what makes the label safe the moment the worker's consulta upgrade (#65) starts setting the flag. **That branch cannot be exercised on live data today** (PNCP 503/timeouts, worker lane owns it), so it is pinned by tests and verified in the browser against a fixture. **59 files / 946 tests** (922 at `main`), lint, typecheck, build green; `apps/web/**` plus this row, branched fresh off `db1c97d`, no merge. **Browser evidence, real Chrome, both widths** on a throwaway `/dev` route rendering Sci's two tenders plus the `estimated_value = 0`, `confidential_budget = true` and control cases (route deleted before the commit): at **390 px** no horizontal overflow, mobile card layout, every price cell "—", the new sum line, and "Valor sigiloso" on the flagged card; at **1481 px** the table with both value columns "—". Across all five cases: **0 occurrences of `R$ 0`**, 6 × "Valor não informado", 2 × "Valor sigiloso", `sumNote` only on the control. The one console error is a browser extension injecting `bis_skin_checked`, not ours. **Needs Sci — copy, brief §5.** (a) **New string shipped**, `radar.opportunity.items.noSum` = *"Não há soma a mostrar: ao menos um item deste edital está sem valor publicado no PNCP."* Alternatives: *"Os itens deste edital não têm valor publicado."* (plainest, but does not explain why a partially-priced list also has no sum) and *"O órgão não publicou os valores destes itens, então não há soma para mostrar."* (warmest, longest). It deliberately does **not** say "sigiloso": the sum line's statement is *these rows have no published price*, which is not the same claim as *the órgão declared the orçamento sigiloso*, and only the latter is evidence-backed. (b) **`radar.card.confidential` ships unchanged as "Valor sigiloso"** — Sci suggested "Sigiloso". Three readings: **"Valor sigiloso"** (shipped; parallel with "Valor não informado" beside it, fits the 390 px meta line, but does not attribute the decision); **"Orçamento sigiloso"** (PNCP's and Lei 14.133/2021 art. 24's own vocabulary, so a MEI who has read an edital recognises it — but introduces a second word for what the label above already calls "Valor estimado"); **"Sigiloso — o órgão não divulga o valor"** (safest under rule 3 because it attributes the decision to the órgão rather than to us; wraps to two lines on a card). **Not fixed here, needs a migration card:** `tenders.confidential_budget` is `boolean default false`, but `_confidential()` in `worker/licitaqui/tenders.py` deliberately returns `None` when PNCP sent neither field — *"absence of evidence is not 'public'"* — so **the schema collapses "unknown" into "not confidential" before anything reads it**, and no screen can ever tell the two apart. Making the column nullable is its own PR (CLAUDE.md), and the web change above is correct either way: today's `false`-for-unknown lands on "Valor não informado", which is exactly what we know |
