| 2026-09-23 | Persona journey tests (Playwright, `apps/web/e2e/`) | in review | https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/74 | **A live defect, found by the journey itself: with a list on screen, switching tabs and applying filters do nothing.** The address changes to `?group=check` or `uf=RJ`, the list stays the previous one and **no request leaves**; it only works again on a full document load, or once the snapshot expires (30 min). Cause in `app/radar/radar-screen.tsx`: the effect that writes the snapshot depends on `[key, data]` and is declared before the one that loads, so on a client-side navigation it fires with the **new** key and the **old** data and stamps the previous list under the new search's key; the loader finds a direct hit in `restoreList` and returns without asking for anything — and because `shown.current === restored.snapshot.tenders` it does not even re-render. Minimal fix, **not applied here** (outside the card, and `radar-screen.tsx` is a hot file): carry in `Data` the key the list was read with and `if (data.key !== key) return` in the writing effect; cheap defence in depth, make `restoreList` refuse a direct hit whose `snapshot.group` disagrees with the group asked for, which is the check the `auto` path already makes. Both journeys are recorded with `test.fail()` — they run, they fail on purpose today and they go red on the day of the fix. **The suite:** 22 tests in 5 files, project `journeys`, hermetic — every `/api/**` answer comes from `e2e/fixtures/radar-api.ts`, typed against `lib/radar/contract.ts`, with no database, no worker and **no PNCP**, which was the card's requirement (`pncp-itens` opened five times this morning and a suite that goes red with PNCP's weather is ignored exactly when it matters). 220/220 green over ten consecutive runs. One flake found and root-caused on the way: an unhandled `route.fulfill()` rejection on a page that had navigated left the request hanging and the screen stuck on "Consultando o CNPJ…" — fixed in the fixture, not retried away. Project `accounts` (E3/Telegram and U2/magic link for Seu Jorge) is written and **skipped with its reason in plain sight**: it needs a published environment, a session cookie and an inbox — Seu Jorge's half has never run end to end, and nothing here pretends it has. `pnpm test:e2e` is a new script and **stays out of `pnpm test`** (59 files / 946 tests, unchanged); `ci-e2e.yml` runs only the hermetic lane, advisory until it has a week of green. Minor finding: the locked Documentos block on the tender screen points at a bare `/conta/criar`, with no `?next=`, and loses the edital the person was reading |
| 2026-09-23 | Radar navigation: the group chips and the filter row do nothing with a list on screen | in review | https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/75 | Found by #74's persona journeys, which shipped two `test.fail()` tests holding the broken behaviour; both are ordinary assertions now, because a `test.fail()` left behind after the fix goes red and teaches everyone to ignore it. **Confirmed on production before changing anything** (390 px and 1280 px, CNPJ 36955612000185/BA): after clicking Verificar the URL says `group=check`, `aria-current` is still on Compatíveis, the cards still read COMPATÍVEL, and `sessionStorage` holds `36955612000185|BA||check` whose snapshot is **`group: "compatible"`** with twenty Compatíveis rows — one tab's list filed under another tab's key, in the live product. **Cause:** the effect writing the snapshot depends on `[key, data]` and is declared **before** the one that loads, so a client-side navigation runs it with the **new** key and the **old** data; `restoreList` then finds a direct hit for a search nobody ran and the loader never asks the route for anything. Under a minute that is **zero requests** and not even a re-render (the snapshot holds the very array on screen); past a minute the revalidate path fires one request for the **snapshot's** group, which changes nothing either — both observed. **Two guards, not redundant:** `Data` carries the `listKey` it was read for and the writing effect bails when it disagrees with the key on screen (**the fix** — it covers every axis of the key, which is what the filter row needs); and `restoreList` refuses a **direct** hit whose `snapshot.group` disagrees with the group asked for (**defence in depth, group axis only** — `ListSnapshot` does not carry the filters it was read with, so a snapshot filed under the wrong UF is indistinguishable from a right one there; it also self-heals a poisoned entry left in a live tab by the old build, because the fetch that follows overwrites it). Reordering the two effects would have worked today and gone on being a trap, because it makes the cache's correctness depend on the order two `useEffect` calls are written in. **#57/#62 untouched and still asserted**: coming back restores three loaded pages and the scroll with `calls.tenders` and `calls.cnpj` unchanged — same key restores, a different key fetches. **A third journey was added** beyond the card's two conversions, for the narrow case *between* the guards: an `auto` → explicit-group navigation whose rows do not change, where `restoreList` matches sideways and the screen has nothing to re-render — doing nothing there would leave the state stamped with the old key, so the snapshot would never be re-saved where that URL looks for it and `rememberScroll` would write to a key nothing was stored under; mutation-checked by removing the clause and watching her land **6267 px** from where she was. **59 files / 948 tests** (946 on main), lint, typecheck and build green; **23 journeys, 115/115 at `--repeat-each=5`**, zero flakes; `ci-web.yml` untouched. **Not fixed here, needs a card:** the locked Documentos block in `opportunity-view.tsx` links to a bare `/conta/criar` with no `?next=`, so creating an account there loses the edital — the same shape as #68 |
| 2026-09-23 | `NOTIFICATION_JOURNEY.md` — the three journeys that end in a message | in review | https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/76 | Documentation only; the four delivery defects are the parallel lane's. Magic-link sign-in (7 states), Telegram linking (11), the weekly digest (13 — every skip reason in the code today), each state with what is true in the system, what the user sees **in the app**, what the user sees **in the channel** and how they recover. The four defects are one defect: **the product has states a person cannot observe**, and a confirmation living only on a web page the user has already left is not a confirmation — success belongs in the channel being set up, failure in the channel that still works. **Where the two known defects really are:** defect 1 fires when you *ask* for the link, not when you click it — `pages.verifyRequest` is registered as `/conta/criar?enviado=1` and `@auth/core` appends the incoming search to it, so `enviado` parses as `"1?provider=resend"` and the "Link enviado" card never renders; and defect 2 kills `/ajuda` and `/pausar` too, so a user who pauses **is** paused and never told (the digest is unaffected — the worker enqueues its own jobs in snake_case). **Two findings nobody had reported.** (1) The magic-link e-mail is Auth.js's built-in **English** template — no `sendVerificationRequest` is overridden, so `@auth/core` sends `Sign in to www.licitaquiapp.com.br`, no reply-to (brief §1 wants `contato@`), link valid 24 h, which no screen says. (2) **Nothing reaches Telegram unless `TELEGRAM_DELIVERY=send`** — unset is `dry_run`, and a dry run is deliberately `completed`: it burns the weekly quota and writes `alert_deliveries`, so a silent founders week looks exactly like a delivered one and the tenders it did not send are excluded next Monday. Also: `alert_deliveries.opened_at` is read by the Gate 0 open-rate card and written by nothing, so that gate reads 0 forever. **Follow-ups for Sci** — all Portuguese copy in §7 is drafted and waits on approval (brief §5, §2.2); where does a name come from for a magic-link account (§2 forbids inventing one; the fix lane owns the mechanism); is `TELEGRAM_DELIVERY=send` set in production; should `/conta/alertas` claim "Telegram conectado" before the bot has answered. §9 lists the four `/admin` additions, cheapest first — the `telegram.skipped`-by-reason card needs no new writes and would have shown the digest defect on the first Monday. |
| 2026-09-23 | Four delivery defects: nothing ever told a user it worked | in review | https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/77 | All four found on production by Sci, all four the same shape — **state set in one place that never reaches the place that needs it**. **(1) Every e-mail sign-in landed on a dead page.** `pages.verifyRequest` carried `?enviado=1` and `@auth/core` joins its own parameters onto that value with a *bare concatenation* (`${pages.verifyRequest}${url.search}`) — unlike the `signIn` and `error` branches beside it, which both do `includes("?") ? "&" : "?"`. Production served `302 → /conta/criar?enviado=1?provider=resend&type=email`; `enviado` then parsed as `"1?provider=resend"`, the comparison against `'1'` was permanently false, and the **"Link enviado" card — written, translated, correct — never rendered**. Reproduced before and after: the broken URL renders the bare form, the clean one renders the confirmation. Fix is that **no `pages.*` value may contain a query string**, now enforced by a test; the "we sent it" signal comes from Auth.js's own `type=email` (`lib/auth/verify-request.ts`), with `enviado=1` kept as an alias so internal links and the `seu-jorge` journey keep working. `AUTH_PAGES` moved to its own module purely so the invariant is testable — `lib/auth/index.ts` constructs NextAuth, which the unit suite cannot import. **The same `?`-for-`&` pattern elsewhere:** swept the whole tree; exactly one other real instance, `app/conta/actions.ts` `redirect(`${next}?estado=…`)` ×2, where `safeNext` permits any local path *including one that already carries a query* — not live today (both call sites pass bare constants) but reachable, since `saveCompany` is a `'use server'` POST anyone can call with `next=/radar?cnpj=1`. Both now use a new `withQuery()` that joins correctly. `lib/radar/client.ts`'s `withParams` is provably safe (every caller passes a path built by `editalPath()`) and was left alone. **(2) The bot could not confirm a link, ever.** The web enqueued `{template, userId, chatId}`; the worker reads `user_id`/`chat_id`, so **every** `send_telegram` died on `ValueError: send_telegram payload needs a 'user_id' or a 'chat_id'` and retried until its four attempts were gone (production jobs 19267, 19268). Blast radius is wider than linking: `/ajuda` and `/pausar` die identically, and `/pausar` is the worst of them — `alerts.active` is set *before* the reply is queued, so the person **is** paused and is never told, and pauses again. The weekly digest was never affected; the worker enqueues its own jobs in snake_case. **Fixed in the producer, and the consumer stays strict.** The producer was wrong against its own documented contract — `lib/jobs/index.ts` already said the payload is `{template, user_id \| chat_id}` while the `Reply` type sent neither. Teaching the worker to accept both spellings is one line and would have made this class of drift *silent*; that `ValueError` is the only reason anyone found it. Translation now happens once, in `sendTelegramPayload()`, beside the other functions that mirror a Python counterpart. **The drift alarm is `contracts/jobs/send_telegram.json`** — a schema neither language owns, read by `lib/jobs/contract.test.ts` and `worker/tests/test_job_contracts.py`; the Python side asserts **both** directions, because a test that only fed the handler a correct payload would have passed on the day of the bug too. **The existing DB tests asserted the camelCase payload** — they pinned the producer against itself and never against the consumer, which is exactly the gap. **(3) A magic-link user could never receive a digest.** Google supplies `users.name`; the e-mail magic link never collects one, so every account U2 exists to serve had `name = NULL` and was skipped **permanently and silently** from both the digest and the `/start` confirmation. Chosen fix: **the greeting is now a `[[se: tem_nome]]` block** — `Bom dia[[se: tem_nome]], {{nome}}[[/se]].` renders "Bom dia, Sci." or "Bom dia.", both correct Portuguese, **no sentence changed, so no new copy to approve**. A blank value was never an option (`MissingPlaceholder` by design, templates README §3). **Deriving a name was rejected on the rules, not on taste**: from the e-mail local part or from the company is the same act — putting a name on somebody who never gave us one (brief §2.2), and the address route additionally puts an e-mail inside a message body (§12). Asking for a name at signup adds a field at the highest-drop-off moment and does nothing for the accounts that already exist, including the one that found the bug. Documented as templates README §9. **(4) The skip reason named the wrong field.** `no_company` was raised when *either* the name or the company was missing, so Sci's account — company `JKL EVENTOS`, perfectly good — was logged as `no_company`, and the cause took an hour instead of a minute. These strings reach `events.props` and `/admin` groups by them. `no_company` now has **exactly one** cause; the name cause is *eliminated*, not renamed, so nothing can report it again; and a third meaning hiding in the same `or` (`who.user_id is None`, which is not a company problem at all) now correctly raises the `no_recipient` that already existed for it. **Also fixed, found by this work:** `e1`/`e3.db.test.ts` scoped emails, chat ids and secrets by `RUN_ID` but **not the reply job key** — `reply:<update_id>` counted from a constant, and `jobs_dedupe` is a *shared* index, so two runs of the suite deduplicate against each other's rows and one reads back the other's payload. Exactly CLAUDE.md's "a task-scoped prefix looks isolated and is not"; each run now takes its own band and `cleanup()` deletes it. **62 files / 976 tests** (968 at branch point), lint, typecheck and build green; worker `ruff` clean. **Production verification is incomplete and it matters:** the redirect is verified against live `www.licitaquiapp.com.br` (before *and* after), but **defects 2–4 are verified by test only** — reading `.env.local` is blocked by the permission system, so I have no production DSN and no bot token, and I did not work around it. **Nobody has yet confirmed `telegram.sent` vs `telegram.dry_run` in production.** `COMPLETED_EVENTS = (EVENT_SENT, EVENT_DRY_RUN)`, so a dry run **consumes the weekly quota and writes `alert_deliveries` while sending nothing** — if the worker process did not read `TELEGRAM_DELIVERY=send`, every fix here is invisible on Monday *and the system reports success*. The one query that settles it: `select name, count(*) from events where name in ('telegram.sent','telegram.dry_run') group by name;` — **run it before Thursday.** **Needs Sci / carded, not done here:** the delivery mode belongs on `/admin`, but the web cannot read the worker's environment, so the honest card is a data-derived card counting those two events, and it extends a closed `GateKey` union O1 owns; the Auth.js magic-link e-mail is **in English, with no reply-to** ("Sign in to www.licitaquiapp.com.br") on a Brazilian product for MEIs, which is a launch-day problem and needs Sci's own approval, not an agent's; `alert_deliveries.opened_at` is read by the Gate 0 card and **written by nothing**; the worker inserts into `events` with raw SQL, so the closed event-name catalogue binds only the web and one table holds both `telegram_linked` and `telegram.skipped`, which will bite the first `group by` |
| 2026-09-23 | No Telegram message could render in production (worker image) | in review | https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/78 | **`worker/Dockerfile` never copied `templates/`, so every render raised `TemplateNotFound` — for the life of the product.** `licitaqui/templates.py:42` resolves `TEMPLATES_DIR` as `<package>/../templates`, which in the container is `/app/templates`, a directory the image did not contain; the Dockerfile copied `pyproject.toml`, `licitaqui/` and `evaluation/` and nothing else. Across the whole `events` table `telegram.sent` = **0** and `telegram.dry_run` = **0**: not one message has ever rendered, on any path — the linking confirmation, `/ajuda`, `/pausar`, the weekly digest, the empty-week digest. Independent of the four defects fixed in #77, which were about jobs enqueued wrongly or skipped early; this one means that even when everything upstream is correct there is nothing to render. **The fix is one `COPY` line. The rest of the PR is about why it was invisible with every check green**, which is the part that can recur: `pytest` runs against the *working tree*, where `worker/templates` sits next to `worker/licitaqui`, so no test importing this package from a checkout can observe a missing `COPY`; and `docker build` only proves the Dockerfile parses and `pip install` succeeds. So the guard runs **inside the artefact**, in the existing `docker build` job between the build and the push — `docker run --rm licitaqui-worker:<sha> python -m licitaqui.selfcheck` — and an image that cannot send is never published. `licitaqui/selfcheck.py` walks every telegram template id the worker can be asked for (now declared once as `telegram_alerts.TEMPLATE_IDS` instead of living in nine `if` branches) and **renders** each twice, flags on and off, with no database and no network; because it renders rather than listing files it also catches a template deleted or renamed, front matter drifted from the body, a stray `{{ nome }}` that would be delivered verbatim, and an unresolved `TODO(Sci):` in something sendable. `status: draft` is deliberately not a failure. Verified both ways against a reproduction of the container layout (no Docker on this machine): **exit 1** on the pre-fix COPY set, **exit 0** with `COPY templates/` — the check that would have failed today, not one that documents the rule. `tests/test_selfcheck.py` (13 tests) pins the checker's own failure modes and pins `TEMPLATE_IDS` against the files on disk in both directions; one case reads the Dockerfile as text and is the half that needs no Docker daemon. **Audit of everything else resolved relative to the source tree: only `config.ROOT`** (`<package>/../..` → `/` in the image), and it is inert — it is used solely by `resolve_secret` to look for gitignored env files *after* the environment has been consulted, and Easypanel passes the environment directly. Noted, not changed: narrowing secret resolution is a behaviour change and does not belong in a hotfix the night before founders week. **Follow-up for Sci, not this PR: production verification is blocked on the Easypanel redeploy.** CI's `docker build` on `main` succeeds at Build, Login and Push and fails only at "Tell Easypanel to redeploy" — the host is behind Cloudflare and GitHub's runner appears blocked, while the identical `curl -fsS -X POST` from a laptop returns `200 Deploying...`. So merging publishes the image and the worker does **not** pick it up. Once Sci triggers the redeploy, enqueue `send_telegram` with `{"template": "start-linked", "user_id": 5}` (Sci's own account, the only linked chat; `eligible_users` returns exactly `[(5, 'basico')]`) and read `select type, count(*) from events where type like 'telegram.%' group by type`. **A completed job is not evidence** — a dry run consumes the weekly quota and writes `alert_deliveries` while sending nothing; `telegram.sent` is what proves the running process read `TELEGRAM_DELIVERY=send`, `telegram.dry_run` means Easypanel's value is not reaching it. Rendering `start-linked` through the real `build_reply_context` path for a recipient with `name = NULL` — which is user 5 — produces a clean greeting with no gap, so **#77's `[[se: tem_nome]]` is confirmed** as far as it can be off-production. |

| 2026-09-23 | Privacy policy describes GA4 — and the CNPJ is going to Google | in review | https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/80 | GA4 `G-1LVNSD23FX` went live through `GTM-5V6M75R7` this evening; measured, not assumed — the published container went from 332,030 bytes with no `G-` ID to 378,522 with one, and `last-modified` moved. Policy updated in §§2, 4, 5, 8, 10, 11, 17: `_ga` is a third-party cookie with a per-visitor id and a two-year life, so §10's "apenas cookies necessários" and §5's "com dados agregados" were both false the moment the container was published. §10 now names both cookie categories, states plainly that the metrics **are not anonymous**, and gives two ways to refuse them. **The finding that matters more than the wording: `/radar/edital/…?cnpj=51885242000140&q=papel` is a real address** (pinned in `radar-view.test.tsx:180`) **and GA4 sends `page_location` as the full URL**, so Google is receiving the CNPJ and the search term of every visitor — while §3 of this same policy calls the CNPJ of an MEI personal data "porque costuma conter o nome da pessoa" and §7 promises we never send the CNPJ to the model. The form never worked without JavaScript — `fetch` submit, `noValidate`, no `action` (pinned by a test) — so the dialog costs nothing that existed; its unit assertions moved to a new `signup-form.test.tsx` rather than being dropped. **`Sheet` never returned focus on any exit path**: the restore sat in a cleanup guarded by `panel.isConnected`, and `open` going false detaches the panel before that cleanup runs, so the guard was false on the close path too — the docstring had promised it "on every exit" since the drawer shipped and nothing had ever asserted it. Now an effect keyed on `open`, with a journey that presses Escape and the close button and asserts focus is back on the trigger. `Button` gains an **`outline`** variant (label 6.16:1 on surface, border 5.78:1 on ivory, 5.30:1 on the hover fill — `secondary` reads as "back", `onBrand`'s white border is 1.07:1 on ivory). Green: lint, typecheck, **1101 unit**, `next build`, **65 journeys**; `scrollWidth == clientWidth` at 390/440/700/900/1024/1280 with the dialog inside the viewport at all six. **Twenty mutations, each asserted applied on disk before its result was read** — two of them found weak tests rather than confirming strong ones: the reachability test passed with the hero's CTA deleted (the sticky header satisfied it; now scoped to `main`), and removing `w-full` from the shot changed nothing because Tailwind's preflight already caps images. An independent review of the first commit (§4b) found the `sizes` attribute was claiming 52vw for a 464px box and fetching the 1080px variant, that the e2e bound on it could not fail, and that the 1.6MB PNG in `public/` was being deployed twice — all three fixed here. **Follow-ups: card D14** — seven approved strings stopped rendering and none was deleted (`pillars.title`, the four `pillars.items[*].title` verbs, `pain.facts[0..1]`, `pain.source`); `R$ 272,6 bi` was the page's only sourced market-size figure and the `R$ 397/mês` competitor anchor survives in `founderValue.comparisonRows`. **Card D11 rescoped to `/`**: `ExampleRadar` left this page (it still renders on the Landing, so nothing is orphaned). **For Sci:** the `/radar` CTA borrows `account.screen.radar` and would read better as a `foundersPage` key of its own, and `faq.columns` is now vestigial two-array structure that is flattened on render. |

Measured, before → after: **1280** h1 5L@60px / p 3L / img 464×291 → **h1 4L@40px / p 4L / img 593×371**; **1024** 5L / 4L / 421 → **4L / 4L / 476**; **900** 5L / 4L / 365 → **4L / 4L / 414**. `0.85/1.15` is the end of that lever, measured: `0.82/1.18` buys 16px and costs the headline a line, `0.78/1.22` costs the subtitle one too. **The shot's remaining size is bounded by `Wrap`'s 1120px measure, not by the split** — at a 1280px measure the same ratio draws it 685×428 with the headline still on four lines, but the hero would then sit 80px wider a side than the sticky header and every section below; **that is Sci's call and is not taken here**. **(2) Two shots, one download.** `radar-preview-mobile.png` (780×1688) renders below **540px**, the desktop master at and above it — the breakpoint is where the desktop shot stops being legible, not a round number: resampled from the master it reads at 700 and 520px and does not at 400, and in the one-column layout the shot is `100vw − 40px`, so a 500px floor is a 540px viewport. **`<picture>` with a `<source media>`, not two `next/image` toggled with `hidden`: measured, Chrome fetches a `display:none` `<img>`, so the `hidden` pair ships both files at every width.** Verified in the network panel at 390/430/440/539/540/900/1024/1280 — **exactly one request each**, mobile below the line and desktop above. Next's own `getImageProps` art-direction recipe; no `<link rel=preload>`, because a preload cannot be made viewport-conditional without fetching both. **Two real defects found while doing it:** `priority` is deprecated in Next 16 and through `getImageProps` emitted **neither** `loading` nor `fetchpriority` — the shot was eager only by browser default and never high priority (now both, asserted); and `quality={90}` was **silently coerced to `q=75`**, because Next 16 defaults `images.qualities` to `[75]` and rounds anything else to the nearest allowed value. `next.config.ts` now allowlists `[75, 90]`; the 1200w variant costs **64 KB → 92 KB** for the sharpness Sci asked for. That assertion lives in a journey, not a unit test: vitest renders without `next.config.ts` and always sees `q=75`. **(3) The band** takes both title and body from `pillars.items[i]`, drops the plan labels, opens with its `pillars.label` eyebrow (no `<h2>` — `pillars.title` stays orphaned), and draws **an icon per cell** — `company · tender · margin · alert`, not the retired `search · tender · money`, which `Pain` leads with in the same order one scroll below in an identical blue square; overlap is now one glyph. Four across **from 1120px, never from 900**: at 900 the four-up measure is **166px, about 21 characters**; at 1120 it is 214px. **(4) Refunds moved into the FAQ** as its first row, with every sentence unchanged. **This contradicts the placement table in `docs/legal/faq-cobranca.md:70`** (*"Página da Oferta, abaixo do preço"*) — an accordion hides the CDC art. 49 withdrawal right until clicked. Sci was shown the table and decided it on 2026-09-24; `docs/legal/` is untouched. `refunds.ctaLine` stays beside the price in the dialog. **(5) The closing CTA** is rebuilt as a two-column `SectionHead` with a light price card and moved between `Timeline` and `Faq`. **The draft's five ticked lines are deliberately absent**: four of the five exist nowhere in the catalogue, and *"Todos os estados do Brasil"* is a coverage claim nothing substantiates — a test now guards against them being invented. **(6) Section eyebrows are blue** (`tone="accent"`, never a `className`); `--color-blue` on Ivory measures **5.78:1**, recorded in `contrast.test.ts`. The two inside the brand panel stay `inverse`. **(7) Alternating full-bleed section grounds**, on the `<section>` and not on `Wrap`, with `divided` dropped wherever the ground changes. **`fill-muted` and not `surface`, and that is a deliberate substitution:** white was tried first and three sections — the band, the price chain, the closing offer — carry a white panel of their own that vanished into it. `fill-muted` is the same order of change against Ivory (**1.082:1** vs 1.066) and puts those panels above their ground; three new ink/fill pairs pinned. Rhythm **48/64/80**, on this page's local `Section` only — `app/(public)/page-parts.tsx` is shared with the Landing and stays at 40/48 (parallel-lane rule). **(8) The signup dialog is full-bleed below 560px** at `h-dvh`: `position: fixed` measures the layout viewport, which does not shrink when an iOS keyboard opens, so `max-h-full` left the submit behind it. Verified at 390 and 440 — panel fills the screen, the form scrolls inside it, the submit is reachable and submits; Escape and the close control both still dismiss and both still return focus. Unchanged at ≥560px. Green: lint, typecheck, **1124 unit**, **86 journeys**, `next build` with **`○ /fundadores`** still static; `scrollWidth == clientWidth` at 390 and 440. **Nineteen mutations, each asserted applied on disk before its result was read.** Two found weak tests rather than confirming strong ones: the pillars-divider journey **cannot see the column count** — with four-up restored at 900 and the border variants keyed to 1120 the drawn counts are still `[2,2]`, so a direct column-count assertion was added and mutation-proved; and the "loads eagerly" test asserted the *absence* of `loading="lazy"`, which passed while the markup carried no loading and no fetchpriority at all. **For Sci: `ruler.label` shortened to "Exemplo real" leaves the four price figures with nothing on this page naming what was bought** — `papel A4` appears only in `radar.*` keys, which are other pages. Not a false claim, so not a blocker, but unattributed figures on the page taking money. **Follow-ups: card D14 extended** with `hero.promises[0..3]` and `pillars.items[*].plan` — eight approved strings that no longer render, none deleted; note the band no longer says which paid plan each capability belongs to. `signup.db.test.ts` failed once mid-session on a non-empty `founders_list` and passed on a rerun — the same shared-fixture isolation already carded under D7, reproduced on a stashed tree, not caused by this branch. |

The alternation assertion was a *local* property and any parity-preserving arrangement satisfied it. The `Sheet` boundary was tested at 390 and 900 but **not at 559/560**, the two widths it turns on; both added, and the misleading `height < 800` bound removed rather than strengthened — measured at 800px and 1600px tall, with and without `min-[560px]:h-auto`, the panel is viewport-driven and identical either way, so **no height assertion can discriminate that class** and the comment now says so instead of implying otherwise. **Also:** `#tool` is a header nav target with no `<h2>` (`pillars.title` is orphaned by Sci), so the landmark announced as a bare "section" — it now takes its accessible name from the eyebrow already on screen via `aria-labelledby`, no new copy; and `Sheet`'s docstring claimed it owns the close control, which belongs to the content — below 560px the scrim is fully covered, so a future `centre` caller without a close button would have **no pointer dismissal at all**, and the only thing that would have warned them said the opposite. **Card D14 extended again (e):** `ruler.alt`, `ruler.scaleStart` and `ruler.scaleEnd` render nowhere since the price-chain commit and were missed when D14 was rewritten in this same PR; the comment above the price-chain eyebrow still justified it by "it names the commodity the four figures are about", which `ruler.label`'s shortening made false — the comment now records the state, and the decision on the missing attribution is Sci's, graded higher than the previous row graded it. **Nine mutations, each asserted applied on disk before its result was read**, every one turning its intended test red. | 2026-09-24 | `/fundadores` confirmation: a promise nothing keeps, a seat that shouted, a share with no mechanism | in review | https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/106 | **The delivery diagnosis is the part that is live and wrong, and this PR does not fix it — it stops the page claiming it.** `founders.confirmation.nextWhatsapp` ("Mandamos uma mensagem no seu WhatsApp…") is past tense and false: F1 enqueues `send_whatsapp` correctly, `handlers.py:47` **does** import `whatsapp.py` so the registry is complete, and `WORKER_JOB_KINDS` unset means the container claims every kind — the stop is `WHATSAPP_DELIVERY`. With it off, `send_text` returns a dry run **before** normalising the number or resolving any credential, so the job runs all four consent gates, renders the message, writes `whatsapp.dry_run` and finishes **`done`**: `jobs.status` reads success, the delivery log has a row, no error is recorded, and no socket is opened. A dry-run founders week and a delivered one differ only by an event *name* — the shape `NOTIFICATION_JOURNEY.md` flagged for Telegram the day before. **The switch is off deliberately and must not simply be flipped:** the only Evolution instance on the server is `flowdeski-scn-real-estate`, another product, so `send` today would deliver the founders welcome from that product's number (card **E4** carries the real sequence). **I could not read production config or the `jobs` table** — env files are denied by policy and there is no `psql` — so the ranking is from code and docs, not rows; the confirming query is in the PR. Two consequences for E4: enabling is **not retroactive** (already-`done` jobs never re-run, and `jobs_dedupe` is partial — `where status in ('queued','running')` — so the founders already signed up must be re-enqueued), and a prior `whatsapp.dry_run` does **not** block the later real send (`_already_sent` matches only `whatsapp.sent`). **No e-mail either, and none was ever wired:** `worker/templates/email/founders-*.md` are rendered and sent by nothing. Copy is Sci's (legal brief §5) so the string is **untouched** in `pt-BR.json`; the confirmation stops rendering it behind `WHATSAPP_WELCOME_IS_DELIVERED`, a rendering decision, one token to restore, both settings covered by tests. CDC art. 30 on a page taking money. **Seat:** was 34px mono against a 20px heading — the number was the announcement and "Vaga garantida" its caption; now quiet, body face, heading leads. **Share:** `founders.confirmation.share` invited a share with no link, button or address — the **sixth** instance of the shape CLAUDE.md names. `ShareSeat` adds a copy-the-link control on `common.copy`/`common.copied`, which already existed and were rendered nowhere; the address is shown in full and selectable so a refused clipboard costs a gesture, not the link. **No WhatsApp share:** it needs a label and a message body written to the recipient and neither exists — `confirmation.share` is addressed to the reader, so reusing it would put approved words in an unapproved context (card **D15**, wording Sci's). **1110 unit, 73 journeys, build green.** Every new test mutation-checked with the mutation asserted as applied — which caught **two of my own tests passing while the behaviour was wrong**: `toContainText` on the shared address matched the substring while the button copied a relative path, and an ellipsised URL at 390px defeated the documented clipboard fallback with every assertion green. An independent review (§4b) then found four more: `border-line` at **1.30:1** on the new controls (the defect `contrast.test.ts` exists to prevent, and cannot see — it checks token pairs, not which token a component picks) → `border-field-line` + `min-h-control`; a page-overflow assertion that **could not fail**, because a `fixed` dialog never contributes to document overflow and `Sheet` sets `overflow:hidden` besides → replaced with bounding rectangles scoped to the dialog, verified live; a comment claiming coverage that did not exist; and `role="status"` being implicitly `aria-atomic="true"`, so each copy re-announced the whole confirmation twice → explicitly `false`. **Follow-ups for Sci:** (1) **E4 is a launch blocker** — a LicitaQui Evolution instance, then credentials, then one verified message, then `WHATSAPP_DELIVERY=send`, then re-enqueue, then flip the flag; (2) **D15** — the two share strings; (3) `foundersPage.founderValue.benefits[2]` promises the opening notice arrives by WhatsApp — a future promise, not a past claim, but on E4's critical path before 08/10; (4) reopening the dialog after a successful signup shows the empty form again (`Sheet` returns `null` when closed, so `SignupForm` unmounts) — pre-existing, but the share control is the first real reason to reopen it; (5) `signup.db.test.ts` failed twice mid-session on its own `founders_list must be empty` precondition with the count changing between runs while another lane wrote to the shared test database — its `cleanup()` deletes only its own `@…invalid` domain while `expectEmptyList()` requires the **whole table** empty, so any foreign row fails it; green on the final run and unrelated to this diff, which touches no file under `apps/web/lib/` |

One flat image cannot be both. Now two: `radar-full.png` (1600x1000) as the base carrying the silhouette, at default quality because nothing in it is legible at any quality; and `radar-preview.png` re-cut to **688x620** as an inset at 58% width — 58% of 593 is 344, and 688 source px at 2x is 344 logical, so it lands at **exactly 1:1** and the app text renders at its own size. The inset carries the argument: the four compatibility checks with page references and PRECO-ALVO DE COMPRA / R$ 14,60 with the sentence defining it. Measured in a browser: base 593x371, inset 344x327, scrollWidth minus clientWidth = 0. A first attempt cut the inset at 590 logical px into a 344px box (0.58x, text at 7.5px) — the measurement caught it and no assertion would have. The inset is its own `<picture>` with a transparent-pixel fallback, not an `<img>` hidden by CSS, so a phone loads 70 bytes rather than 99KB; there is a new guard for that. |

| 2026-09-24 | The hero shot becomes a link to the Radar, not a lightbox | in review | https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/111 | Sci: *"what I was thinking if the person clicked into the image, could opened/expand — your suggestion?"* A link to `/radar` instead. Somebody who clicks a product screenshot is asking to see more of the product, and the better answer is their own editais live rather than a 2x JPEG — the action already exists as the button beside it. A lightbox would also need a real accessible name, a role, a focus trap, an Escape **and a label string, which is Sci's**, for a third interactive element in a hero whose job is two actions. The wrapper is `aria-hidden` with `tabIndex={-1}`: the adjacent "Ir para o Radar" button is already the accessible control, so a second focusable link would announce the same destination twice and add a tab stop nobody needs. Mouse convenience over decorative artwork (`alt=""`), deliberately invisible to the keyboard rather than half-exposed. Guarded by a test asserting both halves — clickable, and not a tab stop — mutation-checked by removing `tabIndex`. |
| 2026-09-24 | `/fundadores` signup dialog on phones: an error nobody could see, Back as a data-loss gesture, and a keyboard fix that was not one | in review | https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/110 | **The live one, and it was losing sign-ups.** On a validation error `signup-form.tsx` moved focus on `done` **only**, `components/field.tsx` renders the message as a bare `<p>` (correct `aria-invalid`/`aria-describedby`, which announce nothing until the *control* is focused, and nothing focused it), and the form's deliberate `noValidate` switches off the browser's own scroll to an invalid control. Three conditions, all true: at a phone's visible height somebody who mistyped their e-mail was left at the submit with the message **off the top of the screen** and the button reverting from "Guardando sua vaga…" to its idle label — which reads as nothing happening. Fixed by focusing the first errored **control** and centring it, so one move announces, scrolls and lands the caret in the right field; no `aria-live` on the message, which would then double-announce. **Measured, this does not reproduce at Playwright's chromeless 390×844** — the form is 1186px there and the field cannot leave the screen — so the new tests run at 390×560, an iPhone SE-class *visible* height, and are generous: with the keyboard open every phone is smaller. **Back** now pushes a history entry with **no URL change** (`pushState(null, '')`, Next copies its own `__NA` marker, asserted: same address, same document, no reload) and `dismiss()` pops it, so the gesture people use to leave a full-screen panel closes it instead of leaving `/fundadores`; the finished signup was lifted into `SignupSheet`, which never unmounts, so a seat number and share link survive a close that used to destroy them. **The keyboard comment was wrong and is corrected**: `dvh` is defined by retractable browser chrome and explicitly *not* by the on-screen keyboard, so this morning's `h-dvh` fixed the `p-4` gutter and the toolbar case, not the keyboard. `Sheet` now publishes `--sheet-h`/`--sheet-top` from `window.visualViewport` (the only API that reports a keyboard) and the `centre` panel consumes them with `100dvh`/`0px` fallbacks, `items-start` below 560px. **Playwright cannot raise a software keyboard**, so that half is *not* verified by the suite: what is asserted is the mechanism (published from `visualViewport`, consumed by the drawn box, released on close) and the keyboard itself **needs a real handset**. Also: the card-in-a-card is gone below 560px (324px measure → 358px at 390, one shared `PANEL` constant so the confirmation cannot drift from the form), and the dialog has a **visible title** below 560px — the same element and the same accessible name, carrying the label of the trigger that was pressed, so no new copy. A `mr-auto` on that heading looked right and did nothing (`not-sr-only` resets `margin: 0`); a screenshot caught it, and its position is asserted now. Every new test mutation-checked with the mutation verified in the file first. **A review that did not write the diff found six more, all with the suite green** (CLAUDE.md §4b), fixed in a second commit: (1) **a regression this PR introduced** — lifting `done` into the sheet made `SignupForm` focus the confirmation on *mount*, and React flushes a child's effects before its parent's, so `Sheet` recorded a node inside its own panel as the opener and the next close restored focus to a **detached element**, i.e. `<body>`; both halves fixed (the form announces only on the transition, and `Sheet` refuses to record anything inside the panel) and the join is now walked by a test, which neither half's own test did; (2) **the headline defect was still live in one field** — `sells` is `optionalText(200)` and its `tooLong` reaches the client, but the `vende` control rendered no error and was in no list, so a long paste gave the idle button back and nothing else; the comment saying "nothing can fail there" was simply wrong; (3) the two consent boxes had no `aria-describedby`, so focusing one announced the consent sentence and nothing about the rejection — two of seven fields getting a third of the promised behaviour; (4) **`max-[559px]` is not the complement of `min-[560px]`**: Tailwind v4 emits `@media not all and (min-width: 559px)`, so [559, 560) matched neither and the header fell back to `justify-content: normal` (verified in the built CSS; ~12 more instances in `page.tsx`, cosmetic, not swept here); (5) `history.back()` only *queues* a traversal, so the panel stayed on screen with its close button live — **two taps walked off the page**, reproduced in a test that dispatches both clicks in one frame; (6) Forward and reload-while-open leave an orphan entry that swallows one Back: kept, and written into the docstring with why the state-marker alternative is worse. Follow-ups carded in plan §5: **D16** `viewport.interactiveWidget` (the Android half, one line, site-wide — deliberately not ridden along) and **D17** the sheet still forgets what was *typed* on every close (Escape and the close button always did; Back now behaves the same) |
| 2026-09-24 | D11 (the Landing example goes stale on 30/09) | in review | https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/113 | **Chose neither route the card suggested, because both re-date the defect or cannot be made honest.** Sourcing the panel from the database at revalidation is legal — §3.3 gives public pages ISR, §3.1 step 1 *is* "the route reads the entity from Postgres", and `/` already reads it twice (`openTenderStats`, `founderSeats`) — but it cannot produce the group badges: `Compatível`/`Verificar`/`Palavra-chave` are claims about a fit with a company, `listTenders` needs a `CompanyMatch` plus a keyword, and there is no example company — the three current ones were hand-checked in the POC answer keys, so live rows would need an invented profile. And the panel is the page's spine: `alerts.items` and `opportunity.tender`/`.deadlineValue` are **approved copy** naming these same three editais at 30/09, so live data up top leaves two worlds on one page. **What shipped:** the facts stay frozen, the clock stops being one. `EXAMPLE_NOW` deleted, `now` read once per render and passed to all three cards, and `mayShowUrgency` (§2.2 rule 6, already shipped) drops the countdown and relabels the line *"Data anterior 30/09"* once each card's session hour passes. No new copy — `card.previousDeadline` existed for exactly this; `pt-BR.json` untouched. **The guard is the point:** `example-radar.test.tsx` renders the whole Landing at five moved clocks (24/09, 29/09 23:00 BRT — already 30/09 in UTC, so it pins the product timezone — 30/09 08:00 BRT where one of three has closed, 01/10/2026, 01/01/2030) and asserts per card. The calendar-dated guard Sci asked for would have failed every build from 01/10 by design and only ever bitten after the rot. Mutations asserted applied: re-freeze → 5 red; clock past every deadline → page-level assertion red too; freeze **one** card → 5 red; a `Date` export under another name → red. **Follow-ups:** (1) **D11b** — refreshing *which* editais the example shows needs real PNCP rows (no DB credentials in an agent session, `.env.local` deny-listed) and Sci's approval for the three strings that name them; (2) found by the independent review and carded there too — `opportunity.deadlineLabel`/`.deadlineValue`/`.deadlineNote` still render "Proposta até · 30/09 · 08:30" for the same edital with no clock, and `alerts.items[0].note` says "encerra 30/09", both dated only by `opportunity.source`; (3) removed a now-dead `export { deadlineLabel }` from `tender-card.tsx` (its only consumer was the test line this branch deleted); (4) **not mine:** four `*.db.test.ts` tests timed out at 5 s on one run against the shared Neon database and passed on the next two — `plan-limits`, `list-cache`/poll; nothing in this diff touches the database. `signup.db.test.ts` did **not** fail here. |

Mutation-checked by renaming a cited card, which reddens it by name. A register nobody can break is one that drifts — and a drifted register is worse than none, because it is believed. |

| 2026-09-25 | Finished a WIP lane closing three audit findings (phone abuse, rate-limit durability, security headers) | in review | https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/120 (depends on https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/119) | Inherited 23 uncommitted files from a stopped session, committed verbatim as insurance (`4b06364`); nothing in it had been reviewed or re-run. **Split #119**: `db/migrations/0007_rate_limits.sql` off the application change, per CLAUDE.md — #120 depends on it, but does not need it first: `checkDb()` treats the missing table as any other unreachable database and falls back to the in-memory limiter, self-healing once the table exists. **Traced, not trusted, all three findings**, each with a mutation-check whose application was asserted before reading the result: the WhatsApp guard (removed → dedup test reddens, `201` instead of `200`), the rate-limiter fallback (catch block changed to rethrow → all four fallback tests reddened with `DATABASE_URL is not set` instead of decisions), and the headers (dropped `X-Frame-Options` → 3 of 4 real-build journeys reddened). **Found and fixed a real defect in the WIP's own headers test**: `security-headers.spec.ts`'s CSP-violation console filter (`/content security policy|refused to/i`) also matched an unrelated MIME-type refusal — `next start` outside Vercel's edge 404s `/_vercel/insights/script.js` and `/_vercel/speed-insights/script.js`, and the new `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` then blocks the browser from executing that 404 page as a script — reddening the suite on every non-Vercel-hosted run for a reason it was never meant to catch. Narrowed to require "content security policy" by name. **Open, not verified from this sandbox**: whether `next.config.ts`'s headers ever reach Vercel's own `/_vercel/*` responses in real production (those routes are documented as edge-served, which would mean no) — worth a quick check on the next preview deploy, since if they do, Vercel Analytics/Speed Insights would silently stop reporting. Green: `pnpm lint`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm test` (1155/1155, including the new WhatsApp-dedup case against a real test database), `pnpm build`, `pnpm test:e2e` (127 passed, 6 skipped — the `accounts` project, which needs a real deployment and self-skips by design). |

| 2026-09-25 | #120 — merged `main`, fixed one stale assertion and one flaky shared-counter test | in review | https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/120 | GitHub reported `CONFLICTING`; the merge was in fact clean, but `main` had changed `signup.ts` underneath this branch (E6's `queued_email` CTE) and a clean *textual* merge proves nothing about behaviour. Two real problems, both found by running the suite rather than reading the diff. **(1)** `blocks a second signup that reuses a WhatsApp number` asserted `jobs` had length 1 under the `founders:<id>` key — E6 now enqueues `send_whatsapp` **and** `send_email` under that same key, so one signup legitimately queues two. The dedup itself was never broken: the duplicate attempt still queued nothing. Re-asserted **by kind** rather than by count, so the test says *why* two is right and would still fail if a third sender were wired in silently. **(2)** `rate limits a burst from one address` failed once and passed on retry — the worst shape a failure can have, because the retry looks like a fix. Cause: the durable limiter counts in the shared `rate_limits` table, the test's client address was the **fixed** constant `198.51.100.200`, and `resetRateLimits()` deliberately deletes only keys *this process* wrote, so two concurrent runs share one counter and the sixth request 429s in whichever arrives second. Exactly the per-run-id rule in `CLAUDE.md`, which this suite was not following — `DOMAIN` is a task constant too. Every client address in the file is now derived from a per-run id. 1155/1155 web tests, typecheck clean, lint one pre-existing unrelated a11y warning. |

| 2026-09-26 | Hardening the joins — three guards, after a morning of config faults | in review | https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/133 | Every fault on 2026-09-26 was configuration, not code, and each surfaced only once the one before it was fixed: a stale image with no `send_email` handler, then `EMAIL_DELIVERY` unset (which rendered, logged `dry_run` and finished **`done`**), then `AUTH_EMAIL_FROM`, with `RESEND_API_KEY` waiting behind it. | 2026-09-27 | Neon cost — the compute was never allowed to sleep | in review | https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/134 | Neon ran **48.93 CU-hrs in 7 days** and the supplied analysis blamed unindexed deletes, unbatched inserts and a runaway "migrator" worker. **None of that survived contact with the repo.** `DELETE FROM tenders WHERE starts_with(id, $1)` — its "CRITICAL" finding, 1120 calls — exists in exactly one place: `worker/tests/conftest.py:454`, a **function-scoped** pytest fixture running twice per test across 8 tests against the isolated `TEST_DATABASE_URL_C1` database. 1120 ÷ 16 per session ≈ 70 sessions; there were **95 `ci-worker` runs** in the same week. No production path deletes from `tenders` at all. `migrator` is the **DDL role** (`db/migrate.py`), and `migrate.yml` runs only on pushes to `main` touching `db/migrations/**` — seven files ever. **The actual cause is arithmetic**: Neon suspends after **300 s** with no connections (spec §5.1) and consumers polled every **120 s**, so the timer never fired. `0.25 CU × 168 h = 42 CU-hrs` against 48.93 observed, with the compute at 0.02 of 0.25 vCPU throughout — we were paying for an endpoint that never slept, never for queries. Poll interval → **1800 s**, which is the floor rather than a guess: the scheduler already wakes the database every 30 minutes for `sync_open_tenders` and `sweep_tender_values`, so 60 or 120 minutes would save nothing and would delay retries, stale the syncs and fire the 08/10 broadcast up to two hours late. User-facing latency is untouched because the web calls `POST /wake` on enqueue. Both pinning tests were rewritten to assert the **relationship** (`poll > NEON_SUSPEND_SECONDS`) rather than a number, since pinning 120.0 is what made the wrong value look correct for weeks; mutation-checked by restoring 120 s, which reddens both, restore byte-identical by `md5`. CI: the Neon suite comes off every pull request and runs on merge plus nightly at 03:00 BRT, with a `run-integration` label to opt a PR back in — and `image` keeps building on PRs (`needs` plus an `always()` guard allowing `skipped` but not `failed`), because skipping it would have taken `licitaqui.selfcheck` down with it, the one check that catches an image which builds and cannot render. Two incidental fixes: the nightly and a `main` push shared a concurrency group with `cancel-in-progress`, so a 03:00 merge could be cancelled and never publish; and the workflow header still claimed the image is never pushed, which misled this very investigation into assuming the running worker matched `main`. All four proposed indexes rejected with reasons in the PR. **E14** cards the one real per-row write loop (`queue.enqueue`) — explicitly not a cost fix. |
| 2026-09-27 | Layer 1 — one file holds the product's numbers, and a guard proves every other file still agrees | in review | https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/136 | Before changing the Essencial and Pro prices and the seat count, the blast radius had to stop being a manual hunt. A price is written by hand in ~30 places: the copy catalogue, 5 worker templates, and 4 legal documents where it is a **contract clause** and therefore has to be prose, not a placeholder. That duplication cannot be removed — so `docs/product.json` holds the values and `apps/web/lib/product.test.ts` proves the rest still agree. **Changing a number there renders nothing differently; it turns the test red and names every stale file.** Two directions, because a price change fails in two ways: a *stale site* (five of six updated) is caught by asserting each required file still contains the current value; an *unknown amount* is caught by asserting every `R$ …` literal in those trees is either a plan price or a named exception — each exception spelling out what it is (the price-ruler illustration, a competitor's R$ 397/mês, the R$ 272,6 bi market statistic), so the list cannot quietly become a bag of numbers appended to when a test went red. Also pins two things that have drifted before: the promo's successor price **is** the Essencial price, and the opening date in `product.json` matches the `08/10` the copy prints. Mutation-checked in both directions: changing `essencial.brl` to 59 reddens 3 tests and names all four stale files; appending an unclassified `R$ 77` reddens the classification test. | 2026-09-27 | Two Neon follow-ups carded so they are not rediscovered | in review | https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/135 | **H3** — verified that all 18 DSNs resolve to one endpoint, `ep-flat-firefly-acai77cp`: `neondb` and all 15 `licitaqui_test*` databases share a single compute. | 2026-09-27 | A `short_title` that was the whole objeto — 541 characters on a card | in review | https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/138 | Sci spotted one Radar card rendering as a twelve-line wall. **My hypothesis was wrong in the useful direction**: I assumed the pipeline gave up and the page fell back to `objeto`. It does not. `titles.build()` **writes the entire objeto into `short_title`** whenever the model is unreachable or its answer is rejected — reproduced against the real row, `deterministic_title()` returns 541 characters and they are stored with `short_title_source = 'ai_fallback'`. The shape of it is the part worth keeping: `needs_model()` judges that exact string **unfit to show a user** (>80 chars, opens with contract boilerplate), the model is asked, its answer is rejected as `too_long` at 121 characters — and the rejection branch then ships the 541-character string the same function had already condemned. **The negative test result was computed and thrown away.** `MAX_TITLE_CHARS = 120` was applied only to the model's answer, never to the fallback, though its own comment says the number was chosen to match where the web truncates. And `STALE_SQL` then froze the row: not null, versions match, basis matches — so the hourly sweep never revisits it. Fixed at source with `fit_for_display()`, mirroring `trimObject` in `format.ts` including its 0.6 word-boundary rule so both sides shorten identically, applied at all three fallback exits. Bounded again in `displayTitle` via a `SHORT_TITLE_CEILING` distinct from the caller's `max` — the two limits are different things: `max` is how much prose a screen has room for, the ceiling is the contract with the worker. The docstring that claimed a short title is "short by construction" now says why that was false. **No CSS `line-clamp`**, deliberately: `format.ts` already argues truncation beats clamping because a clamp hides that there is more and leaves the whole string in the accessibility tree, and `opportunity-view.test.tsx:402` asserts its absence on purpose. Both fixes mutation-checked; restores byte-identical by `md5`. 73 worker title tests, 39 format tests. **Existing rows are not healed by this** — `/tmp/cl/poisoned-titles.py` sizes them and re-queues by nulling, since bumping `RULES_VERSION` would re-title every tender in the table for a problem affecting a subset. |

| 2026-09-28 | B17 — the Radar held half the editais it promises, because the feed it reads is a feed of *changes* | in review | PR pending | `/v1/contratacoes/atualizacao` windows on `dataAtualizacaoGlobal`, so an edital published once and never touched again never re-enters the window. Measured 2026-09-27: PNCP 137, Radar 57 for `q=saas`, and **54 of the 72 in-scope misses had never been updated after publication**. Re-measured 2026-09-28 across all modalities: PNCP calls **24 541** open, we hold **13 581** — **55.3%**. `reconcile_open_tenders` asks the other question, daily at 04:00 BRT, partitioned by UF with **no `stop_at`**. UF is the partition that provably loses nothing: the 27 totals sum to 26 036 and the unpartitioned national figure is also 26 036. Each partition's `total` is read *before* it is walked, and a slice past 60% of PNCP's 10 000-record window **raises** — past the window PNCP does not error, it stops answering, so a sweep that finds the limit by reaching it has already returned an incomplete set. **The prerequisite was larger than the card knew**: `UPSERT_SQL` never wrote `search`; only `sync_items`' roll-up did, so **8 905 of 29 089 rows (31%) had no vector and every one had zero items** — an exact correlation, and those tenders matched nothing in the Radar whatever their objeto said. The expression now has one definition (`search_vector.py`) used by `items.py`, `db/seed.py` and the upsert; it was two byte-identical copies each asking the next person to keep them in step. **Two reviews before merge, and both earned it.** The first found the suite was already red — an existing guard grepped `db/seed.py` for the literal this change deletes — and that `search_vector` was not the one definition at all: the generated SQL had been baked into `tenders.py` with `pt_unaccent` hard-coded twice while three docstrings claimed otherwise. The second found the real blocker: **212 ms per single enqueue against Neon**, so the first cycle's ~39 000 follow-ups would take **138 minutes** against `STALE_RUNNING_SECONDS = 3600` — the queue would requeue the job underneath itself, and with `WORKER_CONCURRENCY > 1` start a second national sweep beside the first. `queue.enqueue_many` (one `unnest` statement) makes it **0.77 ms — 274×** — and closes the shape **E14** cards. It also predicted the backfill would blow the 30 s `statement_timeout`; **measured on production it is 2.79 s for all 8 905 rows**, so that one did not survive contact and the comment now carries the measurement instead of the guess. Also fixed from review: `complete` meant "nothing raised" and would have certified a cycle that stored nothing (a stretch of 204s zeroes both revealing numbers at once) — it now requires coverage; the backfill can no longer take the cycle's event row down with it, and runs even when planning fails since it needs no network; UFs are filtered *before* counting, so a one-UF rerun no longer inherits the national `expected`; an unknown UF raises instead of sweeping nothing and reporting success; the client is closed. **And one of my own tests was lying**: it claimed the `search is null` gate prevents vector narrowing — mutation proved it does not (removing the gate leaves it green; the *expression* is what protects, because it always recomputes objeto **plus** items), and the docstring now says what it proves. A claim of mine was also unsupported: I justified the daily cadence with "an endpoint we have watched return 429s" — every recorded 429 here is OpenRouter or Telegram, and ADR-0001 measured `/api/search/` at **0% failure across 52 requests**; removed from three places. **B20** cards the next partition split, because `PartitionTooLarge` named B18, whose own acceptance says the partitions *still clear* the window. Verified against real data, rolled back: RR ingested 43/43 with 86 follow-ups, a rerun inserted 0 and moved 0 `updated_at`. **1172 tests, 42m35s** — the full suite, which is a different claim from the 847-test 45-second subset. **Follow-ups: the two `CLAIMS.md` rows this card owns are not closed by the code** — they close by evidence, after a cycle has run: re-run the `saas` comparison for ≥95%, and re-read the landing's "Hoje no Brasil" figure (13 262 printed against ~25 936). And the card's second named example, `04876389000194-1-000044/2026`, cannot match `q=geoespacial` — its objeto does not contain the word. |

| 2026-09-30 | Custo Neon (compute) | Feito | — | Medido: 5,5 h/dia de compute, das quais ~4 h eram a cauda de 5 min que a Neon cobra depois de cada acordar, e só ~1,5 h era trabalho. Depois `sync_open_tenders` e `sweep_tender_values` passaram de 30 para 60 min e o poll de 1800 para 3600. **Acordares: ~48/dia → ~24, cerca de um terço a menos de compute.** Custa até 1 h de frescura em vez de 30 min; nada que uma pessoa espere muda, porque prioridade 1 continua a chegar por `POST /wake`. Os testes que fixavam os números passam a fixar invariantes: o poll nunca mais curto que a entrada mais lenta, e `sweep_tender_values` preso à cadência do `sync_open_tenders` — duas entradas no mesmo instante são uma ligação e uma cauda, e se se separarem a poupança cai a metade sem mais nada dar por isso |

| 2026-09-30 | D25 (3)(4) | PR aberto | [#193](https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/193) | A barra de acção no fundo dos três ecrãs de edital, e a contagem de triagens por baixo do botão. O motivo é a frase do Sci de 29/09 — *"a decisão está abaixo da dobra"*: num telemóvel o ecrã do edital é o banner, o título, os selos, o cartão do prazo e o Objeto **antes** do único botão para que o ecrã existe, e cada "Mostrar mais" nos Itens empurra-o mais para baixo. Slots por ecrã, todos com palavras já aprovadas: edital → *Ver triagem por IA* / *Ver a triagem que você pediu* + *Itens e Documentos*; triagem → *Até quanto ofertar* + *Edital*; preço → *Ver plano Essencial*, **só quando a banda está bloqueada**, que ali é a única acção seguinte. O primário lê os mesmos dois valores que o CTA da página, por isso a barra não pode vir a dizer o que o botão por baixo não diz. **A (4) não precisou de cópia nenhuma**: `plans.quota.visitorLeft` e `plans.quota.basicLeft` estavam aprovadas há muito e **não eram lidas por ficheiro nenhum** — o defeito com nome deste repositório — e passam a renderizar, por baixo do botão e nunca dentro da etiqueta, a partir do resumo que o `app/radar/layout.tsx` já lê uma vez por rota. Cala-se em vez de dizer algo aproximado: sem contagem em plano ilimitado, silêncio para um plano medido sem frase própria, e o estado esgotado fica para o `radar.screening.quotaTitle`, porque os ramos `=0` nomeiam um 5 e um 2 literais e o B27 é o que custa imprimir um limite de memória. **A ficha dizia barra `fixed` e a barra é `sticky`, de propósito.** A primeira versão era fixa mais uma constante `BAR_CLEARANCE` de padding — e **essa constante não existe**: medida na página construída, a barra tem entre **69px e 144px** consoante haja legenda (+23,5px), a etiqueta do primário quebre linha (quebra a 360px e abaixo, +32px) e a própria largura (+12,5px a 320px). `pb-28` são 112px, portanto um leitor com conta que já pediu a triagem, num Android de 360px, tinha os últimos 12,5px da página debaixo da barra — 32px a 320px. Um número maior só muda a largura onde erra, por isso mudou a **forma** (§4d): um elemento `sticky` fica no fluxo e reserva exactamente a sua própria altura, seja qual for, e o tapar deixa de ser improvável para ser impossível. **Três defeitos apanhados pela revisão com tudo verde**, e o primeiro explica os outros: o teste de browser chamado *"a última linha não fica atrás da barra"* media o último `main li` — o último **item**, não o último conteúdo, que está 286px mais abaixo no bloco `mt-auto` a seguir ao `<Record>`. Tinha 286px de folga contra uma barra de 90px e ficava verde com a folga posta a `pb-0` (provado por mutação). O teste unitário ao lado importava o `BAR_CLEARANCE` e verificava que o `main` o continha — passa para qualquer valor. Segundo: o slot da barra no ecrã do preço dizia *"Edital"* e ia para a **triagem**, porque ali o `backHref` é o `screeningHref`; o teste verificava a palavra e nunca o href. Terceiro: o `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)` não acolchoava nada, porque não existe `viewportFit: 'cover'` na app, e o comentário descrevia uma protecção que o produto não tem. **E o browser apanhou o que o `node` não pode ver**: a primeira versão chamava `useAppMenu()` dentro do `opportunity-view.tsx`, que é renderizado pelo ecrã cliente **e** pelo `page.tsx` servidor — 1 414 testes verdes e o Next a dizer *"Attempted to call useAppMenu() from the server"*. O hook vive agora atrás de uma fronteira cliente própria, como o `menu-trigger.tsx` já faz. Quatro jornadas passaram a limitar o localizador ao `main`, porque a página passa mesmo a ter dois links com o mesmo nome acessível abaixo do `lg`; a barra tem spec própria, e essa spec carrega o clique no primário, que de outro modo nunca seria exercido. Mutação com restore byte a byte por `md5`: voltar a `fixed` avermelha **exactamente os seis casos de tapamento** (320/360/390 × primeira visita/regresso) e deixa verdes os outros cinco. 1 414 testes vitest, 165 Playwright, typecheck limpo, lint 0 erros. Nada para o `CLAIMS.md`. **Por fazer, dito para não se perder**: a barra é um `<div>` sem landmark e os dois links repetem nomes acessíveis do `main` abaixo do `lg` — um `<nav>` com rótulo resolve, e o rótulo é uma frase que só o Sci aprova |
| 2026-09-30 | Faixa de preço (correcção) | PR aberto | [#195](https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/195) | **O Sci reparou que só os itens 13 e 37 daquele edital tinham histórico, e a pergunta levou a um defeito sério em produção.** O item 37 é um perfurador de papel de ferro fundido, 100 folhas, que o edital estima em **R$ 168,78**. Dos onze "editais parecidos" que lhe davam preço, **nove eram cadernos, papel higiénico e bobina de papel** — o único perfurador equivalente de verdade, *"PERFURADOR DE PAPEL 02 FUROS DE AÇO FUNDIDO … 100 FOLHAS"* a **R$ 204,00**, tinha a *menor* semelhança do conjunto e era vencido pelos cadernos. O ecrã concluía: pague no máximo **R$ 9,35**. O item 13 falha ao contrário: é um toner Lexmark **compatível** estimado em R$ 129,85 e foi cotado a partir de cartuchos **originais** (o mesmo 50f0z00 original está lá, a R$ 525,80), dando faixa de R$ 256,95–294,49 — cerca do dobro. Num ecrã chamado *Até quanto ofertar*, isto faz alguém propor a perder dinheiro ou a perder a disputa. **A causa é estrutural e o gate existente não a podia apanhar.** A PNCP escreve `<Produto> atributo: valor, atributo: valor…`, por isso dentro de um segmento e uma unidade o andaime de atributos é a maior parte da string e produtos sem relação nenhuma pontuam bem entre si. O `price-band.ts` exige 5 editais e um IQR ≤ 50% da mediana — mede **precisão, não exactidão**: cadernos custam todos R$ 9–15, logo uma amostra só de cadernos é *mais apertada* do que uma correcta, e passa com folga. Nenhum limiar sobre esse teste resolve. Medido em 103 itens abertos de `Gráfico / Escritório` e `Informática / TI` que devolviam comparáveis: **a mediana da fracção que era sequer o mesmo produto é 0%**, e 65 de 103 estão abaixo de metade; uma das quatro faixas da amostra era `Papel Kraft` cotado a partir de guardanapos e fita adesiva. Correcção: `lib/radar/product-key.ts` exige que **todas** as palavras do produto do item apareçam no candidato (direccional — o candidato pode ser mais específico, nunca menos) e que o **grau** coincida quando ambos o declaram (`original` vs `compatível` vale o dobro do preço). Medido depois, pelo caminho de código real: os dois itens do Sci ficam com 2 comparáveis cada e **não mostram faixa nenhuma**, o `Papel Kraft` fica sem nenhum, e numa amostra de **400 itens de todos os segmentos** os itens com algum comparável caem **62 → 14** e as faixas ficam **0 → 0** — uma faixa já é rara ao ponto de 400 itens não encontrarem nenhuma dos dois lados, o que é a própria conclusão. **O custo está dito na ficha e no `CLAIMS.md`**: a cobertura já era ~1% e passa a ser rara ao ponto de não a conseguir medir. É a troca certa — o `radar.price.noData` (*"Ainda sem dados de vencedores para este item"*) é uma resposta honesta e um preço errado no ecrã da proposta não é — mas encosta o **C3** (chave canónica de produto) a ser o que faz a funcionalidade existir, não o que a faz boa. Cópia não mudou e não precisava: *"nos editais em que já temos esses dados"* já cobre. Todos os fixtures dos testes novos são strings reais lidas de produção em 30/09 — fixtures inventadas passariam também no código antigo. 1 443 testes, typecheck limpo, lint 0 erros **A revisão antes do PR apanhou quatro coisas com tudo verde, e três eram minhas.** (1) O extractor de produto só cortava a chave de atributo quando ela estava em minúsculas — verdade em ~18% do corpus — por isso `PAPEL A4 COR: BRANCA` exigia a palavra literal *cor* a todos os comparáveis. (2) Não havia limite de comprimento: medida a mediana do corpus é de **7 palavras** e **65,7% das descrições não têm `atributo:` nenhum**, logo exigir todas era identidade de string, e a maior parte da cobertura perdida não vinha da identidade do produto mas do extractor. (3) O grau lia-se da string inteira, por isso *"compatível com &lt;modelo&gt;"* (8 576 itens) e *"embalagem original"* (1 130) contavam como grau — errado em cerca de dois terços das vezes em que disparava; e *"PRODUTO ORIGINAL OU COMPATÍVEL"* resolvia para `original` pela ordem da tabela. (4) **Nada exercitava o caminho real**: a rota faz mock do `comparablesForItem` e não havia teste de base de dados nenhum. O `comparables.db.test.ts` existe agora — e a primeira versão dele **passava com o gate removido**, porque as fixtures curtas que escrevi não chegavam ao piso de 0,3 de trigrama; com as descrições reais, sem o gate, os cinco cadernos dão faixa de mediana **R$ 9,64** ao perfurador e os três testes avermelham. Mutação com restore byte a byte por `md5`. Uma coisa mais, minha: o cabeçalho do teste dizia *"todas as strings são reais"* e as do `Papel Kraft` tinham sido escritas à mão — trocadas pelas verdadeiras, e a verdadeira `Fita Adesiva … material: papel kraft` apanhou um falso positivo que as inventadas escondiam: fita feita de papel kraft cotava papel kraft. A regra passou a comparar **nome de produto com nome de produto**, e não o nome contra a descrição inteira. 1 450 testes |
