E daí veio a decisão maior: *"o CTA está em baixo, portanto este CTA já não é necessário… os dois CTA vão para o mesmo sítio, e o botão de baixo é o primário."* Por isso **o `lg:hidden` saiu da barra e os botões da página foram apagados** — no ecrã do edital e no do preço, onde o mesmo *"Ver plano Essencial"* era desenhado duas vezes. Tirar o breakpoint só é seguro porque a barra é `sticky`: uma barra `fixed` não tem bordo esquerdo correcto ao lado de um carril cuja largura está em `localStorage`, e uma `sticky` é posicionada pela própria coluna. **Isto fecha de caminho o item de acessibilidade que a ficha carregava** — deixou de haver dois links com o mesmo nome acessível, o `<nav>` com rótulo deixou de ser preciso, e as quatro jornadas que tiveram de limitar o localizador ao `main` voltaram a ser simples. **O aviso de IA foi para o rodapé da leitura**, como o Sci pediu; o teste que o prendia por cima do botão foi reescrito para fixar o que continua a valer. **Uma coisa por confirmar, e é do Sci**: o §2.2 regra 5 diz que o aviso aparece em todos os ecrãs de resultado, e aparece — mas já não está ao lado da acção, e a acção está sempre no ecrã, por isso dá para carregar sem ter passado pela frase. Se a regra também quer dizer *antes de agir* é resposta dele. A linha de custo (`screeningCost` / `screeningCostReady`) **foi com a acção para a barra** em vez de desaparecer com o botão debaixo do qual vivia — é uma das cinco strings que este repositório nomeia como o seu próprio defeito e não volta a não renderizar em lado nenhum; a barra mostra-a quando a próxima pressão gasta uma triagem, e mostra a contagem restante quando não gasta. Mutação nas duas invariantes novas, restore byte a byte por `md5`: repor o `lg:hidden` avermelha o caso de 1280px no browser e dois unitários; repor o botão na página avermelha *"é o único caminho para a triagem"* e *"mantém a acção fora do scroll"*, mais dois da jornada da triagem. 1 450 testes vitest, 165 Playwright, typecheck limpo, lint 0 erros **A revisão antes do PR apanhou seis coisas, e a pior era minha e apagava a própria (4).** As legendas tinham ficado um ternário — custo *ou* contagem — e como o `screeningsLeftCaption` só responde a `visitor` e `basico`, que são exactamente os planos medidos, a contagem deixou de poder aparecer numa primeira visita: a barra dizia *"Usa 1 das suas triagens."* e mais nada, no momento exacto em que saber quantas restam interessa. **As duas suítes não viam** porque nenhum teste punha as duas juntas — os do custo renderizam sem shell, os da contagem renderizam em shell sem `showScreeningCost`, e esse par é uma combinação que o `opportunity-screen.tsx` nunca produz. Agora rendem as duas, e há um teste da combinação real. Mais: os critérios de aceitação do D25 ainda exigiam *"ausente a partir do `lg`"*, o oposto do que foi feito; a nota de acessibilidade que ficava em aberto **foi apagada inteira quando só metade estava resolvida** — a barra continua a ser um `<div>` fora de qualquer landmark, e agora que o botão da página desapareceu é a **única** acção do ecrã, o que é pior do que antes, não melhor (restaurada na ficha; o rótulo do `<nav>` é frase do Sci); o D28 ainda citava a etiqueta antiga, que já não existe em lado nenhum; e três comentários afirmavam o contrário do código. Dois testes eram mais fracos do que o nome: o da ordem passava com um CTA duplicado restaurado, e o do aviso de IA passava se o aviso fosse para dentro do painel de Itens (invisível em Documentos) — ambos apertados, com o aviso agora verificado nos dois separadores, nas duas metades da frase, e num edital cujo id o `pncpEditalUrl` não consegue ler. No browser, o 1280px só dizia *visível*, que passa numa barra simplesmente deixada no fim de um documento longo; passa a medir que está **encostada** ao fundo e que começa onde a coluna começa. E duas coisas medidas que ninguém tinha escrito: a barra ocupa **92,5px** permanentes de cada viewport de desktop, e uma página que antes cabia pode agora ter scroll. 1 452 testes vitest, 165 Playwright |
| 2026-10-01 | D26 | PR aberto | [#199](https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/199) | A lista de conferência que a canvas 11 desenha — *"N de M conferidos"* — passa a existir como objecto. Era o **join** que faltava, e só isso: o `reasons()` do ecrã do edital devolvia um `string[]` de positivos sem veredicto por linha, sem denominador, sem página e sem forma de dizer *"isto não foi lido"*; as findings do ecrã da triagem tinham tudo isso e nenhuma ligação com ele. Agora há `CHECKLIST_KEYS` — dez chaves, fixas e ordenadas — das quais **três são respondidas pelos campos estruturados da PNCP antes de alguém pagar** e sete precisam do edital lido, que é o que uma triagem compra e o que este bloco existe para mostrar. **O denominador não se mexe**: decisão do Sci contra o *"3 de 4" → "5 de 7"* da canvas, porque quem está a decidir se gasta uma triagem tem de conseguir ver as sete linhas que neste momento não dizem nada. Acrescentou-se `Finding.known` para que uma linha que o modelo não respondeu não possa ser contada como conferida através de uma comparação com a cópia — uma reformulação do `values.unknown` passaria a contar não-respostas como respostas, em silêncio. A lista viaja no `TenderOk`, construída **dentro da consulta que já lia a triagem**, por isso a rota não paga uma ida extra à base e o cliente nunca recebe uma leitura que talvez não tenha pago. **O `entrega/local` entra**, e isso não contradiz a ficha: o worker já lê `entrega.local` com citação de página, e o que a ficha proíbe — *"na sua região"* — é uma afirmação sobre a **faixa de preço**, cuja consulta não tem predicado de UF nenhum. **O estado `blocker` não entra, e isso foi medido.** A regra escolhida pelo Sci é que uma linha só é barrada quando a leitura o diz, e os `bloqueadores_pequena_empresa` são `[{ponto, pagina}]` — texto livre que não se liga a linha nenhuma. Ensinar a chave ao prompt foi tentado e avaliado ao vivo, nove execuções no mesmo modelo e sessão, R$ 0,07 ao todo: **sem tocar no prompt 96,6 / 96,6 / 98,3**, nunca abaixo do portão de 95%; com o enum dentro do esqueleto JSON **94,8 / 96,6 / 93,1**, duas em três abaixo; com uma versão mais leve **81,0 / 96,6 / 96,6**. **A pior execução da base bate a média de qualquer das variantes**, por isso nenhuma entra e a **D35** leva o trabalho. Duas falhas são constantes em todas as execuções e são deriva do modelo, não nossas. Ficou um **tripwire** no `checklist.test.ts`: afirma que nenhuma linha é `blocker`, para que a lacuna não se transforme caladamente em *"nenhum edital tem barreiras"* — esse teste deve falhar quando a D35 aterrar. Fica também a nota de método: um `grep -c` no meio de uma cadeia `&&` devolveu 0 e **o código de saída 1 abortou a cadeia**, por isso a primeira medição da base nunca correu e quase foi reportada como feita. 1 469 testes web, 165 Playwright, 874 do worker, ruff limpo **A revisão antes do PR apanhou quatro defeitos com tudo verde, e dois eram sobre o que o objecto diz às pessoas.** (1) **Nada mantinha a leitura fora do fio.** O `screeningAvailability` passou a devolver a análise, por isso escrever `screening,` em vez do `{ready, spent, metered}` reconstruído campo a campo despeja o `ai_analyses.result` inteiro no browser — é **TypeScript válido**, porque a verificação de propriedades em excesso não se aplica a uma referência a variável, e ficava verde em 1 333 testes. A rota não tinha ficheiro de teste nenhum; passou a ter, e a mutação avermelha-o. (2) A lista não estava condicionada nem ao `spent` nem à empresa: passa a ser **pelo `spent`, não pelo `ready`** — o §3.2 partilha a leitura e o §10 aloca-a por utilizador, por isso um edital que outra pessoa pagou está por ler aqui, e as páginas que lá estão foram compradas com a triagem dela. (3) O `cnae` **não conseguia dizer "conferimos, e não"**: uma lista de segmentos vazia tanto quer dizer que não se pesquisou empresa nenhuma como que se pesquisou e o CNAE não cobre este edital — que é o grupo **Palavras-chave** inteiro — por isso toda a gente que chegasse por aí era informada de que não tínhamos conferido um CNAE que tínhamos conferido e rejeitado. (4) O `meEpp` estava preso à PNCP, que **está calada em 15 189 de 47 991 editais (31,6%)**; onde ela não diz nada a linha passa a cair na leitura, que responde e traz página. Mais: as quatro subconsultas `currentAnalysis` passaram a um `lateral` (aquela ordenação não tem desempate, logo em princípio duas colunas podiam vir de linhas diferentes; e são 6 blocos partilhados em vez de 24), quatro asserções que tinham sido afrouxadas para `toMatchObject` voltaram a `toEqual`, e o comentário que dizia *"analysis é null exactamente quando ready é false"* estava errado — uma linha `no_text` é `ready` sem leitura nenhuma, e há uma em produção. Duas fichas novas saíram daqui: o **D27 fica bloqueado no D35**, porque a frase de topo dele é uma contagem de `blocker` e sem o D35 lê *"0 exigências podem te barrar"* em todos os editais do produto; e o **D36**, porque a PNCP e a triagem **discordam sobre ME/EPP em 14 de 27 leituras (52%)** e as duas renderizam, em ecrãs a um toque de distância. 1 474 testes web, 165 Playwright |

Sem tabela companheira. **A decisão que pesa é outra: todos os planos levam linha, incluindo `0` para o `visitor` e o `basico`.** A ausência é a única coisa que esta tabela ainda não consegue dizer sem ambiguidade — a web lê linha em falta como zero, o worker lê a mesma ausência como **sem limite** (`telegram_alerts.py`: *"ler linha em falta como 'zero' … silenciaria todos os fundadores no promocional na semana de abertura"*), ambas de propósito, e o **F6 existe para resolver qual ganha**. Se eu tivesse apoiado isto na ausência — como cheguei a propor ao Sci, apresentando-o como já decidido — teria cimentado um dos lados dessa contradição numa funcionalidade nova a uma semana da abertura. O critério *"demonstrado mudando"* é um teste de base de dados que vira o `quantity` nos dois sentidos sem deploy nenhum; e um segundo teste **afirma a própria ordem de implantação**: aplicada a `0012`, todos os planos reais têm de ter linha, e antes disso não pode haver nenhuma — por isso um estado meio aplicado falha, em vez de negar a faixa a um fundador pagante em silêncio. O `PRICE_BAND_PLANS` foi apagado, não apenas deixado por usar, e o teste da rota da faixa passa a simular o `plan_limits` como tabela em vez de simular o `hasPriceBand`, para que o que está sob teste continue a ser *este plano inclui a faixa*. Mutação: repor a lista congelada avermelha os dois testes que interessam, restore byte a byte por `md5`. Migração validada contra o esquema real numa transacção revertida — as cinco linhas saem como esperado e a contagem volta a 0; nada foi aplicado. **Duas PRs, como o CLAUDE.md exige, e o `pnpm db:migrate` corre entre elas** |
| 2026-10-01 | H3 · B24 · B25 | Feitas | [#202](https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/202) · [#203](https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/203) | **O CI saiu da Neon.** Os dois workflows passam a correr `postgres:18` como service container; `db/ci_databases.py` cria e migra cada banco a partir de `db/migrations` em todas as execuções. Medido na mesma suíte: `integration` **38 min → 1 min**, `ci-web` ~3 min → **1 min**, e **zero** compute da Neon. O próprio comentário do workflow já tinha o diagnóstico: *"1.8 s each against Neon in sa-east-1 where a round trip is ~200 ms"* — nunca foram os testes. Fecha três fichas de uma vez: **B24** (a serialização de todo o repositório desapareceu — os PRs deixam de fazer fila uns atrás dos outros), **B25** (não há como os bancos ficarem para trás das migrations) e **H3** (já não há nada no compute de produção para mover). Devolve também segurança que tinha sido trocada por custo: a integration tinha deixado de correr nos PRs por ser lenta e cara, e volta a correr em todos. **Os 16 bancos continuam na Neon, já sem ninguém a lê-los** — ficha **B31**, de propósito daqui a uns dias: são ~170 MB ≈ US$ 0,06/mês, o dinheiro era o compute e esse já está poupado. O #203 corrigiu uma falha minha que apareceu no mesmo dia: o `NOW` do teste do `neon_usage` era uma data fixa (30/09) e a `main` ficou vermelha a 01/10 — **B23 outra vez**, dois dias depois de a corrigir nos fixtures do radar, agora com guard |
| 2026-10-01 | B32 · B33 (medição) | Fichas abertas, meia tiragem a correr | [#205](https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/205) | **A pergunta era "porque é que a faixa de preço está quase sempre vazia"; a resposta é oferta, não correspondência — e a oferta tinha parado.** Medido a 2026-10-01: o corpo inteiro de preços são **6 277 linhas de `awards` sobre 970 editais**, contra **19 341** editais já encerrados — 5%. E o `sync_tender_awards` tinha **0 em fila**, 0 concluídos em 24 h e o último sucesso a **2026-09-29 07:27 UTC**: 943 feitos contra 916 falhados, todos com `CircuitOpen: circuit 'pncp-resultados' is open`. Por baixo do disjuntor os erros verdadeiros são da PNCP — `HTTP 503`, `ReadTimeout`, `RemoteProtocolError`. **O disjuntor fez o que devia; o que parou foi a fila deixar de ser alimentada**, e isso é a proposta do produto a morrer em silêncio, porque cada faixa que existe vem de uma linha de `awards`. Uma sonda de 25 editais de SP às 15:28 UTC devolveu **25 em 25, zero falhas, 124 linhas, 4,96 por edital**, a ~19 editais/min, e **uma passagem esgotou cada edital** (124 itens marcados `has_award`, 124 linhas; o tecto de 50 por edital nunca chegou a morder). Logo o endpoint serve e a falha é nossa. **O âmbito é menor do que parecia e a minha primeira estimativa estava errada por 10×**: 18 414 encerrados não têm linha de `awards`, mas só **1 835** contêm um item marcado `has_award` — nos outros não há nada para ir buscar. Projecção a partir do rendimento medido: **~9 100 linhas, corpo a ~15 400 — 2,4×, em cerca de 1,6 h**. **2,4× vale a pena e não é uma transformação**: 15 400 itens com preço contra 430 275 em aberto continua pouco, e o filtro de identidade de produto recusa — bem — a maior parte das correspondências. Por decisão do Sci está a correr **metade (923 editais), estratificada por estado** (SP 147, CE 135, MG 119, RS 96, GO 58, RJ 47), porque meia amostra dá mais exactidão à projecção e serve a fase seguinte do **C3**. **A re-medição depois é que é o ponto** — é o número que deve decidir se o C3 vale dois dias, que é o que a própria ficha do C3 exige antes de qualquer corrida completa. Duas fichas: **B32** (nada enfileira o `sync_tender_awards`; alarmar sobre *0 concluídos em 24 h* e nunca sobre *0 em fila*, porque uma fila que nunca enfileira também nunca falha) e **B33** (o atraso em si). Duas lições no `CLAUDE.md`: um `grep -c` dentro de uma cadeia `&&` **aborta a cadeia** quando não encontra nada — foi assim que uma medição de base nunca correu e foi reportada como feita — e **procurar a automação antes de escrever um procedimento manual**, porque se escreveu um guia inteiro de migração para um workflow que já a tinha aplicado dezoito segundos depois do merge |

2026-10-03 · **B36 formatting** · fixed · PR pending · `main`'s CI is red and has been since #212: the pipeline runs **`ruff format --check` as well as `ruff check`**, and every B36 change was verified with only the second. Seven files — all B36's own — needed reformatting, so `ci-worker` fails on `main` and on every worker PR branched from it, for a reason unrelated to the diff under review. This is formatting only: no behaviour, no SQL, no copy. 1 342 worker tests green in the fast half. **The lesson is the check, not the diff**: `ruff check` and `ruff format --check` are two different gates and passing the first says nothing about the second, so `ruff format --check .` belongs in the local loop beside `ruff check` for every worker change.

2026-10-03 · **B36 hotfix 2** · fixed · PR pending · `map_item_codes` could not write either: `tender_item_codes`' INSERT had **8 placeholders against the 7-value tuple** the mapper built, and the `unknown_kind` branch was **absent entirely** — so a NULL `kind` was silently matched against the *materials* vocabulary, where `exact` is more than twice as likely (18.5% vs 7.6%, re-measured today), under a column whose comment says it was copied from `tender_items`. Both come from the same cause as the first hotfix: edits applied in one pass where **only some carried an assertion**, so two silently matched nothing while the INSERT's own edit landed. Third instance today of `CLAUDE.md` §4b's "a `replace` that matched nothing prints success". **And the first test written for this class did not catch it**: it compared the statement's placeholder count against tuples written *in the test*, so a mutation reintroducing the defect left 28 tests green — verified by running it. Replaced with a test that executes `map_item_codes` against a fake connection and counts what `executemany` actually receives, which fails on both mutations (the 7-value tuple, and coercing an unknown kind to `M`) and restores byte-identical by md5. 2026-10-03 · **lint on main** · fixed · PR pending · One `ruff check` error (`I001`, an unsorted import block in the test added by #217) shipped to `main`, so `ci-worker` is red again. One line. **The lesson is the ordering**: `ruff check` and `ruff format --check` have to be the *last* thing before a commit, after the final edit — not a step passed earlier in the session. 2026-10-03 · **B35** (part 2 of the catalogue migration: the schema only) · written, **not executed anywhere**, not pushed · PR pending · `db/migrations/0014_catalog_prices.sql` adds `catalog_prices` (the minimal per-purchase tuple) and `catalog_bands` (one row per code per window: the band, or why there is none). **No supplier column in either table, as a rule rather than an optimisation** -- every price row carries `niFornecedor`, which is a CNPJ *or a CPF*, and ~3.9 M of them collides with `CLAUDE.md`'s *never log CPF* and spec §12; `awards.supplier_doc` masks CPF on write at 23 448 rows, which at 3.9 M rows is a line of code somebody can forget. **And no `raw jsonb`**, breaking `0001_initial.sql`'s stated convention on purpose: `raw` re-imports both the 41-field payload (**9.65 GB** [M], 5.1x the whole current database, $3.38/month at the repo's own measured $0.35/GB-month in `apps/web/lib/admin/neon.ts`) and `niFornecedor` inside it, against this tuple's **~0.24 GB ≈ $0.09/month [I: ~68 B/row]** -- storage is the row shape, not the row count, and the two measured reductions that compound are the 33 unread fields and the collapse to one price per `id_compra` (**-47%** [M: median 1.87 rows per purchase, worst 47.8]). `refused_reason` is **a column, not an absence**, because it is the only thing that makes the dominant fact visible: of 183 exact-matched items with no band, **182 (99%) were refused for spread and exactly 1 for too few purchases**, every code having >=5 and median 379 [M 2026-10-02, the open-item probe] -- the data is abundant and the identity is too coarse. A `check` pins the band to its refusal (all three quartiles and no reason, or a reason and no quartiles), the shape 0013 uses for `tender_item_codes.rule`; `kind` is checked to 'M'|'S'; `matcher` is stored for 0013's reason. **Zero indexes beyond the two primary keys**, each read named: the band computation filters one code's rows by date under the PK prefix (median 379, max 1 649 [M]), the screen reads the current band by that prefix backwards, the refresh groups staleness over the leading columns of ~7 109 codes [M], and counting refusals seq-scans a few thousand rows -- the `(kind, code, purchased_on)` btree an earlier draft proposed would add ~125 MB [I] to a 0.24 GB table to avoid filtering 1 649 rows in memory, which is 0013's own answer about `active`. `catalog_item_code` is kept and **must not become a band key**: CATMAT-level was measured and rejected 2026-10-03 on a pre-registered threshold (2.7x the coverage at a **33% hit rate, p<0.001 below the ~50% a correct quartile band scores**, against PDM-level's 47%, p=0.871). Clocks are stated per column: `purchased_on`/`window_end` are calendar dates in the data's own calendar, `fetched_at`/`computed_at` are UTC, the job's hour is BRT -- so `window_end` is **not** `now()::date`, which between 21:00 and 00:00 BRT names tomorrow. **Not executed**: there is no local Postgres and no Docker here, so this was verified by reading against `0001_initial.sql` for types and 0013 for style, and Neon `main` needs Sci (it applies itself on merge via `.github/workflows/migrate.yml`). Follow-ups: the primary key `(kind, code, id_compra, item_number)` **permits the uncollapsed row set**, so the -47% is the ingest job's discipline and not the schema's -- correctness survives because `priceBand` collapses by its own key, but only if the read passes `id_compra` as `Comparable.tenderId`; `refused_reason`'s permitted values are deliberately **not** constrained, unlike 0013's `rule`, because the vocabulary belongs to the sibling PR writing the ingest, so a typo splits a count silently until whoever writes the first reason closes the set; `supply_unit` and `catalog_item_code` are written by the ingest and read by no screen -- their reader is **C4**'s offline re-tuning, which is why rows are stored at all. The shape of both tables was pinned by Sci across two parallel lanes and implemented exactly as given.

2026-10-03 · **B35 (schema)** · amended before anything ran · PR pending · `0014_catalog_prices.sql` gained a second price column and lost `item_number` from its key, both for reasons found while the sibling lane built the ingest. **`price-band.ts` has two per-purchase rules and they are not the same rule**: `priceBand` (line 382) takes each purchase's `percentile(rows, 0.5)` — its **median**, which may interpolate, because the band publishes only quartiles drawn across purchases; `priceEvidence` (line 319) takes `sorted[floor((n-1)/2)]` — the **lower-middle real row**, because that rung prints the figure beside the words it closed under. The brief for the ingest lane said to store the real row, which would have fed the band the wrong one of the two: for a two-row purchase of R$ 10 and R$ 20 that is 10 against 15, and at the measured **1.87 rows per purchase** the two-row purchase is the modal multi-row case — so every quartile would have sat *below* the arithmetic the back-test measured (56% hit, +0.2% bias), on top of the exact-match population's own −3.6% bias. **Low is the dangerous direction for a number somebody bids against, and it is unrecoverable after ingest** because the lots are discarded. So both numbers are stored, 8 bytes apart. Two smaller corrections in the same pass: the primary key is now `(kind, code, id_compra)`, which makes one row per purchase **structural** instead of the job's discipline, and `catalog_bands.matcher` became `band_version`, because a band is per catalogue code and no item-to-code matcher takes part in computing it. **Nothing has run** — the tables do not exist and no local Postgres or Docker is available here.
2026-10-03 · **B35** (part 2 of the catalogue migration — the **worker half only**) · built, reviewed **three times**, 32 findings fixed, not pushed, no PR · — · The price ingest and refresh: `licitaqui/price_band.py`, `licitaqui/catalog_prices.py`, the price endpoint + `walk_prices` + its own `compras-pesquisa-preco` breaker in `licitaqui/compras.py`, the handler registration, **a `DEFAULT_SCHEDULE` entry in the same change as the handler** (04:40 BRT daily, priority 9), and `scripts/gen_price_band_conformance.py` + `tests/fixtures/price_band_conformance.json`. **No schema, no web code, no copy.** Written against `db/catalog-prices` `ac12c97`: `catalog_prices` keyed `(kind, code, id_compra)`, **`unit_price_median` alongside `unit_price`**, `catalog_bands.band_version`. **The handler cannot run until 0013 and 0014 are applied**, and neither has been executed anywhere. **The `refused_reason` vocabulary, for the sibling lane's `check` constraint, is exactly four values: `no_rows`, `too_old`, `too_few_purchases`, `spread_too_wide`** — `truncated_walk` is deliberately not among them, because a truncated walk knows nothing about the code and a refusal row would supersede a good band; it is counted in `events`. **Round three found three blockers and the one that mattered most was mine alone: `STALE_CODES_SQL` had no top-level `WHERE`.** Every predicate sat after `) last on true`, so it belonged to the LEFT JOIN's `ON` clause — and a failed `ON` condition keeps the left row and nulls the right side instead of filtering — so the statement degenerated to *"the top N codes by demand"*: **no cadence, no cooldown, and `THIN_REFRESH_HOURS`, `DEEP_REFRESH_HOURS`, `DEEP_PURCHASES` and `ATTEMPT_COOLDOWN_HOURS` all dead.** `tender_value.DUE_SQL` and `sync_awards.PENDING_TENDERS_SQL` both put exactly this kind of predicate in a real `WHERE`; this one did not. **Both tests for it asserted substrings of the SQL text**, so both passed on a statement that filtered nothing — the third instance in this card of a test exercising the unit and not the path. Replaced with an **executed** fixture: `STALE_CODES_SQL` is rendered for SQLite by nine substitutions each asserted to have matched (only the LATERAL→`row_number()` one is semantic; the predicates carry through verbatim and that is asserted), run against seven codes in the states that discriminate, plus a depth-0 structural check that the join ends at a bare `on true` and a **mutation test inside the suite** that reconstructs the `ON` shape and asserts it returns everything. Also fixed: **an unreadable `totalRegistros` discarded a full page and wrote a false `no_rows`** — `_as_count` returned `0` for anything it could not parse and `walk_prices` early-returns on zero, so a JSON float or a localized `"30.616"` threw away 500 real rows and manufactured *"nobody buys this"* out of our own parse failure, one upstream type change hitting the whole feed while `refreshed_codes` stayed healthy; it now returns **`None`** and the walk raises in a frame that holds only projected rows, so *unreadable* is distinguishable from *zero* and "cannot raise with the payload alive" still holds. The test that covered it **blessed the bad behaviour** by pairing every bad count with an empty `resultado`; the dangerous pairing (a full page beside a zero count) is now a test. `_as_count` could also still raise on **`'²'`**, where `str.isdigit()` is True and `int()` is not — now `isdecimal()`. And the **two-stage ingest collapse diverges from the authority at two windows, not three**: storage holds one row per purchase, so window 2 collapses `{its lots} ∪ {the stored representative}` and the purchase's other lots are already gone — lots at 200/1000 then 250 store a median of **225** where `priceBand` over all three gives **250**. The docstring said *"across three or more windows"*, which made it look unreachable; it is reachable at two, the premise (0 of 77 multi-lot purchases with differing `dataCompra`, **one page of one code**) is a reason to believe it is not exercised and **not a bound**, and a test now asserts the divergence rather than the agreement so the consequence is written down. Lower severity, all fixed: `is_usable_price(1e15)` was True while `numeric(16,4)` caps at 999999999999.9999, so one junk price made `executemany` raise and rolled back **the whole code's write** — bounded to the column's domain; `is_usable_price(Decimal('10.5'))` was False and `numeric` comes back from psycopg as `Decimal`, so two `float()` calls were all that stood between that and every code being refused `too_old` with the alarm healthy — `Decimal` is accepted; `compute_band`'s defensive collapse took the **representative row** where the authority takes the **group median**, the card's own defect class surviving inside the guard written against it — and it was *unobservable*, a mutation swapping the two passed all 153 tests, so there is now a broken-invariant test whose numbers discriminate (400/500/600 against 200's 300/400/500); a `too_old` refusal carries `n_purchases = 0` and was therefore classified **thin**, the misclassification `TRUNCATED_EVENT`'s docstring refuses to cause, arriving by another door — now treated as deep alongside `no_rows`; `DEFAULT_SWEEP_CODES = 1000` sat **below** the 1 015.6/day its own comment derived, from a cadence the measured median of 379 purchases/code contradicts — the comment now derives ~626/day at the measured split, states 1 016 as the all-thin bound, labels every figure [M] or [I] and explains why the constant is a cap rather than the need; `WALK_DRIFT_TOLERANCE = 5` reads as the judgement it is, against the 0.17 rows its inputs actually imply (§4d). **And the review's structural point, which was right: `price_band.py` pinned the four constants to the TypeScript and the arithmetic only to numbers somebody had typed.** There is now a conformance fixture **generated by `price-band.ts`** — `scripts/gen_price_band_conformance.py`, the same `node --experimental-strip-types` mechanism `gen_catalog_conformance.py` uses, 15 adversarial cases (two-lot, even/odd lots, ties, the cutoff boundary, a `now` on the 31st where `setMonth` rolls, the spread edge, either side of `MIN_SAMPLE`, `priceBand`'s own 40-lot worked example, and a purchase split across windows) with `now` pinned so the comparison is arithmetic and not clocks. It immediately paid for itself: the TypeScript **excludes** a purchase dated exactly on the cutoff day, and the port admitted it — the comparison is now strictly `>` the cutoff day, which is the faithful port for a midnight `dataCompra`, and that divergence is gone rather than documented. The remaining clock divergence is stated accurately: `priceBand`'s default `now` is the server clock (UTC) while `compute_band` is called with `brt_today()`, which is a **different calendar day between 00:00 and 03:00 BRT — 12.36% of minutes**. The BRT date is deliberate and probably right, because `dataCompra` *is* a Brazilian calendar date, but the earlier docstring admitted only the time-of-day boundary and then claimed *"nothing else is reinterpreted"*, which was false. **Mutation-checked on an isolated copy** (repo files byte-identical afterwards by `md5`): predicates back onto the `ON` clause → 5 failures; band reading the representative row → 1; cutoff admitting the boundary day → 2; and the gate-constant drift test still fails from both directions. **`ruff` clean; 1 492 worker tests green in the fast half** (`--ignore-glob="tests/test_integration_*"`, 46 s), **153 of them in `test_catalog_prices.py`** — the integration half was **not** run and nothing was executed against any database. Remaining follow-ups: `REFETCH_OVERLAP_DAYS = 30` is **[I], not measured** (one observation extrapolated 10×) and the 90-day full-depth walk bounds the damage rather than fixing it; the derived page count is a hard stop on one assumption (that `totalPaginas` follows the `tamanhoPagina` we send, one measurement), kept survivable by the attempt marker; the two-stage collapse residual above is **unbounded**, only believed unexercised. Still open and outside this card: the band **read path** (web), the only reader these tables will have, and the six sentences, which are Sci's.

Escaped to `%%`, and a guard now scans every `*SQL*` constant in the module for a literal percent that is not `%%` — mutation-verified (reintroducing it fails the test; restore byte-identical by md5). **And the statement now executes against the real database**, returning codes in demand order (code 13798 referenced by 878 open items), which is the first execution of any of `0014`'s SQL. The remaining unexecuted statements are the ones behind it: the upsert, `WATERMARKS_SQL`'s `filter` aggregate, the row-value `NOT IN` and `make_interval`. 1 500 tests green in the fast half.

2026-10-03 · **B35 hotfix 2** · fixed · PR pending · The first real refresh failed **16 of its first 16 deep codes, every one of them a correct walk**. `walk_prices` refused a walk when `len({(idCompra, numeroItemCompra)})` fell more than 5 short of `totalRegistros`, on the premise that the pair is unique per row. **It is not.** Measured on the live API 2026-10-03: code 8751 page 1 carries two rows for purchase `92930605900002025` item 59, from suppliers `45778439000188` and `26363190000103` at R$ 5,10 and R$ 5,00 — the *cadastro de reserva*, two suppliers awarded one item, which is data and not a fault. Duplicate rates ran **0.08–1.6%** and scale with volume, so no constant tolerance could have worked. The review before the merge named this exactly and asked for one API call to settle it; **I downgraded it to lower-severity and that was the misjudgement** — it was a hard stop on the whole feed. The fault that check was reaching for is a *repeating page*, which is now caught page-to-page in the walk loop where it is observable (measured: consecutive pages share **zero** identities, so an overlap is a fault). What remains is rows against `total`, with a proportional tolerance because `total` itself drifts — one code reported 6 938 then 6 942 twenty minutes apart. **The first version of the regression test did not discriminate**: 100 rows with 2 duplicates sit inside the 5-row floor, so a mutation restoring the old check left the suite green; rescaled to 1 000 rows at the measured 1.6% it now fails the mutation and restores byte-identical. Third time today a test of mine needed a mutation to prove it was worth having. Also confirmed by the same run: the three codes that completed were all refused `spread_too_wide` with **506, 324 and 318 compras** — abundant data, incoherent prices, which is the 99%-spread finding reproduced on live data. 1 501 tests green in the fast half.

2026-10-05 · **B37** · built · PR pending · The feed alarm B35's acceptance required is now **read** by something: a `/admin` card per feed, answering "when did each last do real work". | 2026-10-05 | D37 (cite the past result) + D41/D42/D43/D44/D45 carded | in review | https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/232 | **The card's premise changed two hours before it was picked up and the card did not know it.** D37 said *"link to the edital"* on `PriceSample.tenderId`; **B35 (#229) landed that morning** and moved the evidence to the Compras.gov.br catalogue, so that field now carries `catalog_prices.id_compra` — a **purchase** key, not a PNCP edital id. Both disputed ids are real, the population is 31, and the fixture reader now matches `[?&]compra=` with a comment saying why. `memory: empty-result-is-not-absence` — twice, inside the card whose subject is §4d, and the second time while *correcting* the first. The lesson that got written down was wrong before it was right, which is the thing worth remembering: **a review finding that something cannot be found is not a finding that it does not exist.** And `RAW_ROW`'s `idCompra = 92990906001072026` sits beside its own `codigoUasg = "929909"` in the same dict. **Why an identifier and not a link**, which is the card's "closed with a measured reason" branch: the price payload carries **no `numeroControlePNCP`** [M — absent from the endpoint's own OpenAPI schema and from `RAW_ROW`], so the proposed PNCP link does not exist at all; the one candidate is Comprasnet's `?compra=<17 digits>`, **PNCP's own published `linkSistemaOrigem`** which we already render verbatim on the opportunity screen, and using it here needs two steps that could not be taken — that a price-API `idCompra` shares that namespace [**I**, implied by the shape, never observed on one record] and that the page resolves, which **could not be checked at all because opening it in a real browser on 05/10 returned a bot-detection CAPTCHA** [M]. `pncp.ts` already refuses exactly this: *"a 404 on the portal with our name on it is worse than no link on a page whose whole purpose is trust."* **So the screen prints the identifier** — selectable, with a copy control reusing `common.copy`/`common.copied`. **No new sentence was written**: the label naming the identifier is copy and copy is Sci's, so the accessible name is the digits themselves and a test asserts `radar.opportunity.copyIdContext` (*"o Id PNCP"*) — which would have been **false** here — never appears on this screen. **A defect found on the way**: the API sends `idCompra` as a JSON **number** (`RAW_ROW` records the integer literal), so a UASG beginning with `0` is truncated *before the worker sees it* and `str()` cannot restore it; `070018`, `081102`, `092201` and `092301` are real leading-zero UASGs in the measured set. `compra.ts` left-pads on read, which fixes the citation and leaves `catalog_prices`'s **primary key** wrong — carded as **D41**, with the one query that sizes it (`select length(id_compra), count(*) … group by 1`, never run; the table holds 907 129 rows). **Tests split per §4c**: vitest pins the *mechanism* — the decomposition over real values, the padding, every refusal, and that a purchase id can never build a PNCP URL — and `price-evidence-ladder.spec.ts` pins the *result*: the id on screen, the click, the clipboard, and **`flex-direction` either side of the 520px container breakpoint**, which is the only observable separating *"the container query applied"* from *"the class string is in the markup"*. **Mutation-checked, seven mutations, every restore byte-identical by `md5`**, and the two that paid for themselves are the ones that came back **vacuous**. (1) Loosening the digits-only guard changed nothing, because the only punctuated case was a 20-character PNCP id the *length* check was already refusing — so the character guard was protecting nothing a test could see, while `'12345-6/2026'` pads to `0000012345-6/2026` and would have been printed as a purchase id with modality `23`; cases added at exactly 17 characters. (A `number === '00000'` guard added in the same pass was **removed again**: 0 of the 31 real ids have one and the case justifying it was unreachable behind the length floor — an unmeasured constraint that could only refuse a real id.) (2) **Swapping `@min-[520px]:` for `min-[520px]:` — a *viewport* query at the same number — left the browser suite green**, because 1024px and 390px are above and below 520 in *both* frames: the pair could not see the one thing D29/D30/D32 are about. A case at **560px** discriminates (no rail below `lg`, so the container is 560 − 40 gutter − 32 card = **488px** while the window is 560), and that mutation now reddens **exactly** that test and no other. The rest: dropping the zero-padding → 1; padding anything, inventing zeros → 1; not rendering the citation → 1 unit + **3 browser**; the container query swapped for `md:` → 1; and shifting the UASG slice by one character → **8**, which is the proof that the fixture cross-check is a cross-check and not a restatement of its own offsets. lint 0 errors, typecheck clean, **1 554 passed / 27 skipped**, 7/7 browser. **An independent review (§4b) found the defect that mattered and it was in the evidence, not the code**: the module's central `[M]` claim said *"32 real purchase ids … in `db/seed/fixtures/pncp/*.json`"*, and the repo holds **6**. The true measured set is **27** — 6 in-repo, 21 in the read-only knowledge base — all 17 characters, all decomposing correctly, re-measured by hand before anything was changed. A second review then found that **the correction was also wrong**: `98621905983652025` and `081102`, both deleted as fabricated, are **real** — they sit in the four records whose link uses PNCP's *other* spelling, and the true population is 31. Everything was re-measured by hand a third time before anything was written. The literal table is replaced by a test that **reads the fixtures and compares each segment to a sibling field** (`unidadeOrgao.codigoUnidade`, `numeroCompra`, `anoCompra`) — a cross-check rather than a restatement — matching `[?&]compra=` so the blind spot cannot recur. The one finding that survived both reviews unchanged: the padding would repair *any* length, so `1234567892026` became a confident 17-character citation matching nothing — now bounded to the single lost zero the evidence supports. Follow-ups: **D41** (the ingest truncation, needs a migration so Sci's), **D42** (the link — blocked on one human opening that URL, not on effort), **D43** (the label, **Sci's**, and it belongs with `CLAIMS.md`'s B35 row (the *"editais parecidos" / "editais encerrados"* row, due 17/10)), **D44** (`tenderId` is a purchase id wearing a tender id's name; the rename crosses the generated conformance fixture the worker consumes), **D45** (`CopyCompra` duplicates `CopyId` where a `context` prop would do). `TO_VALIDATE.md` §12 records both the two sentences and the approach doc's open question 4, which D37 is the first thing to have actually needed answered |

Four strings claim identity on this rung and were **suppressed, never reworded** (legal brief §5): `maxTitle`, `evidenceHelp`, `noDataHelp` (services) and — **found by this card's own test, not reasoned about in advance** — `intro`, the *first* sentence on the screen, whose both halves are false there. **A fourth, found by probing the in-flight state rather than by reasoning**: `price-screen.tsx` holds `bandLocked = true` for the whole round trip, so every first paint of a service item drew `won` as a **locked** row and `maxTitle` over a locked value — *a number exists and is withheld*, on an item where none can exist for anybody, and D40 lengthens that window by putting a trigram query in the request it waits on. A service is never drawn locked now; a material still is. Five `CLAIMS.md` rows for what is left: the material half of the rung has no sentence (`fallbackHelp` names *serviços*), nothing replaces `intro`, and `cta` still sells Essencial on an item where no band can exist, a **material** is still drawn locked on first paint (the review priced the justification at **1.08%** of materials having a band at all, so ~99 in 100 see *a number is being withheld* for a number that will not arrive — `price-screen.tsx`'s default and Sci's to change), and the `market` locked row is drawn on every item. **An independent review of the diff found three things I had not** and all three are fixed here: the 2×2 state *band absent + catalogue present* — ~49 461 open materials, the `spread_too_wide` rung — had **no test**, and relaxing the guard to `band === null` alone left the whole suite green; the route-test mocks were `vi.fn(async () => null)`, so every fixture entered through `as never` and `tsc` checked none of them (one had no `source` field at all) — they are typed now and 28 of 29 casts are gone; and `fallbackTitle` (*"Contratações parecidas"*) was drawn **over an empty card**, announcing a list that is not there, on the 92% of service items that return no comparable. It also prompted a `drawable` guard in the view, because the route's one-line guarantee type-checks the forbidden pair at both render boundaries. Two code follow-ups carded rather than slipped in: `comparablesForItem` has **no `kind` predicate**, so a service subject can match a material candidate four filters deep (adding it changes the coverage measured above); and the structural fix is a discriminated `BandState`. **Cost**: `comparablesForItem` (median 412 ms, worst 2 715 ms, no trigram index) is back on the request path for most items, before the plan is consulted — **B21 is load-bearing again**, stated in the route, the module and `PRICE_BAND.md` §0.2. **Verification, split per §4c**: 6 vitest pin the *mechanism* — the rung cannot compute a band, **armed with comparables `priceBand` demonstrably accepts and that asserted first**, so the negative cannot pass for free; 8 route tests pin that the route never puts one beside it, in all four states of (band present/absent) × (catalogue evidence present/absent), including for an entitled caller; 11 view tests pin which strings the markup holds, including that the view refuses a band over fallback evidence *even when handed both* (the types permit it), that it still draws one over catalogue evidence, and that a **material** is still drawn locked in flight; 5 Playwright journeys pin the *result*, one of them holding the band route open to assert the in-flight state, because `environment: 'node'` cannot tell a string in the markup from a sentence a reader reaches. **Mutation-checked, nine mutations, all CAUGHT** — and one of them (`drawable`) was **NOT caught** on the first run, which is how the test for it got written, each asserted as code (exact single-occurrence replacement, md5 changed) with a baseline run first so an already-red suite could not pass as evidence, and every file restored **byte-identical by md5**. One real defect found by my own test while writing it: the route tests used `mockClear`, which leaves a `mockResolvedValueOnce` queued, so a test inherited the previous one's rung — `mockReset` now. **Cost is the one thing left to decide before 08/10 and this PR does not decide it**: B21's trigram index is a schema change and must be its own PR, so this ships without it — `comparablesForItem` on a `force-dynamic` route, before the plan gate, at 120 req/min per IP, with ~95% of the Neon bill already compute (COST.md 05/10). lint 0 errors, typecheck clean, **1 549 passed / 27 skipped**, and the **whole** journeys project re-run green (**178 passed**) rather than only the five new ones — `pnpm test` is `vitest run` and does not include Playwright, which the review was right to flag. `admin.db.test.ts` and `radar.db.test.ts` failed in the combined run on unrelated assertions — three lanes, one Neon test database — and pass alone, 6/6 and 33/33 |

Revisit at §5's 13/10 reading, not before. Docs only — no code changed |

The new one reads the quantity by its **own** query so nothing is compared with itself, then **renders the real screen** with those limits and counts the inputs — a function-level guard would have missed the defect as it was actually written, one `<Field>` in the markup, and `alerts-view.test.tsx` counts no fields at all (it only checks that a plan granting 0 renders none). Eight tests: the field count on the screen, the clamp, an over-long submission, every granted keyword coming back in a `value="…"`, Essencial granted strictly more than Básico, and two copy sweeps. **An independent review (§4b) found the hole that mattered, and it was not in the new guard — it was next to it.** `savePreferences` had **no test anywhere** and no e2e reaches the filters form, so `formData.getAll('palavra')` could go back to `.get('palavra')` and **the whole repository stayed green**: ten inputs still drawn, clamp still keeping ten, `plan_limits` still saying ten, and exactly one keyword reaching the database — E18's symptom restored in full with the new guard reporting success. The code names that trap in two comments and neither was asserted. New `app/conta/alertas/actions.test.ts` drives the real action with a real `FormData` (session, cookies, `redirect` and `db` mocked; the clamp and `readAlertLimits` deliberately **not**), and the test that *claims* the acceptance criterion's "hand-made POST" now lives there rather than being a direct call to the clamp wearing that name. **Nine mutations caught** — `keywordFields` ignoring the row; the boolean clamp restored; the plans card promising twenty; **`get` for `getAll`, the hole above**; the landing sentence reworded to "dez" so the sweep loses it silently; the Básico card over-claiming against its own row; **the form hardcoding one field, the original defect verbatim**; the form bringing back only the first keyword; `readAlertLimits` hardcoding 1 — each asserted applied **as code** (replacement present, original absent, unique before substitution) and each restore asserted byte-identical by md5 (`quota.ts` `98be7d1…`, `pt-BR.json` `3345dd3…`, `alerts-view.tsx` `2cfa4d3…`, `actions.ts` `e1424ab…`). Occurrences counted by splitting the markup, not with `grep -c`, which counts lines and would read ten inputs on one line as "1" (§4b). **Two things the review corrected in my own wording, both §4d:** the sweep runs against the **seeded** `plan_limits` row — CI builds a local `postgres:18` from `db/migrations`, so it pins copy against the *migration default* and a runtime edit to the production row is still unguarded — and the form renders *the keywords the plan grants*, not *all saved keywords*: on a downgrade the extras are invisible and the next save deletes them. Also fixed three false comments the review caught, all load-bearing for the next reader: `quota.ts`'s "## Absence" docblock claimed `plan_limits` has rows for `basico` and nothing else (only `states` is still basico-only; `keywords` has all four and `0006` gave `alert` to the paid plans), and `link.ts`'s note that the worker reads `value` "until its half of E18 deploys" (it shipped in #165). **1642/1670 web green** (28 skipped, unchanged), lint clean but for a pre-existing `fundadores/page.tsx` warning, typecheck clean. **The worker suite was not run**: nothing in this diff touches `worker/`, and its half of E18 already has `test_integration_telegram.py::test_the_digest_matches_any_of_the_keywords_not_only_the_first`, which saves `["guardanapo", "seringa"]` and asserts **both** reach the message — read, not re-run, because three lanes share those databases (B24). **Left undone on purpose:** the fifth criterion, *"says which one matched"*, is **not** built — the digest never names the matching keyword and `partial-digest-item.md` has no placeholder for it. It needs one pt-BR sentence, which is Sci's under legal brief §5, so it is card **E23** rather than a column nothing renders. **For Sci:** (1) that one line for the digest item (E23); (2) the `CLAIMS.md` row stays open by this file's own standard — the path is verified, but no digest built from more than one keyword has reached a handset, and one delivered multi-keyword digest closes it; (3) **two new cards, not follow-ups** — the review was right that "outside this card's scope" is a reason to card a thing, not to leave it in prose: **E24** drops `alerts.value` and `alerts.kind`, both now read by nothing anywhere (grep finds only comments; `kind`'s sole reference in the repo is its own check constraint, and the live table already holds one row with `cardinality(keywords)=1` and `kind='cnae'`, which `saveAlert` cannot produce) — writes first, migration second, or a deploy lands between them; and **E25** is the state select, which is E18 one column over and worse, because it shows `states[0]` and the next save rewrites the array, so a second state is lost silently. E25 needs two decisions from Sci before anyone builds it (how many states each plan gets, and whether multi-state is a feature at all on a weekly digest); (4) **no Playwright coverage** for the keyword form on any plan — `e2e/accounts/ricardo-telegram.spec.ts` drives connect/waiting/recheck and never submits the filters form, and its project skips without `E2E_SESSION_COOKIE`, so I did not add a spec I could not watch pass. The field count is decided in the first render and is pinned in vitest; what stays unseen is **layout** — ten inputs inside the shell's 720px content column — which is D29/D32's shape and is stated in the test's docstring rather than claimed as covered |
| 2026-10-06 | docs: the union-merge rule, written down after the fourth time | in review | https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/242 | **`docs/STATUS.md` is `merge=union`, and §5 said a rebase *"applies both appends cleanly"* — true across lanes, false within one.** A lane appends its row with `(pending)` and edits it once the PR number exists; union replays that edit as a **second line** rather than a replacement, so the stale copy returns on **every** rebase of that branch. Four times on 2026-10-05: B35, D37, and `task/kw-lane` twice. **The deletion is the dangerous half.** One attempt compared the two rows with a whole-file `md5` — which cannot tell two lines apart — concluded they were identical, and would have kept the stale `PR pending` copy; its own `assert lines[a] == lines[b]` refused and the real diff turned out to be that single field. §4b's point exactly: the check belongs inside the thing that must fail, not beside it. Rule added: count your own row after any rebase touching `STATUS.md`, and assert the rows match before deleting either. Docs only |

Three renderings because there are three states, and *"there is no company"* is a claim only an **answered** request supports — `analyzing`, `timeout` and `error` say nothing rather than reading an absence out of a failure, which the first pass got wrong in the opposite direction. `cnaeCount` now counts CNAEs (`main_cnae` + `secondary_cnaes`, deduplicated, in SQL) instead of the 14 segments they reach. The card's named test exists and **fails on `main` — six of seven assertions — printing Sci's screenshot verbatim**. An independent review before the PR found three more behind a green suite: `?? 0` collapsing *unknown* into *zero* (live on every first paint and for 60 s on a first-time CNPJ, and **permitted by the first version of the invariant test**), a `sessionStorage` snapshot from the previous build restoring a grouped list with no grouping — this very defect, re-created at deploy time — and a db assertion that could not fail under the old implementation. `GroupedBy.cnpj` was dropped: nothing read it and it was the one path from an `httpOnly` cookie into page JS and `sessionStorage` (§12). `CLAIMS.md` gains a Closed row — the register never held this claim and it was live on the most public screen it covers. lint 0 errors, typecheck clean, **1 689 passed / 29 skipped / 0 failed**, 196 journeys |

The placement was the part D23 parked as "its own placement question": the card is one `<a>`, a `<button>` inside an anchor is nested interactive content, so `action` is a slot rendered **outside** it, with the 36px reservation derived once from the card's 14px padding, the 44px touch target and its 6px inset. **Measured rather than assumed**: `explain (analyze)` on production shows the target-list subqueries running after the sort and the limit (`loops=21`) and the favourites scan at `loops=1` with the `tender_id` correlation gone from its `Recheck Cond` — the account's rows read once and hashed, 0.017 ms — while end to end the projection sits inside run-to-run noise, **so no figure is claimed for it**. Two new ways a mutation check can lie are written into the PR body: a guard that cannot fail when the new text contains the old (reported "did not apply" on a mutation that had, and left the file mutated), and a `head -26` that truncated the harness's final `assert returncode != 0`, so a mutation the suite **passed** read as OK — caught by the *absence* of the "restored byte-identical" line. | 2026-10-06 | D51 (the Radar list can be ordered: prazo · maior valor · menor valor) | in review | https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/245 | **"Ordenar: prazo" was a label, and the screen said in as many words why a control would have been a lie** — the query ordered by `proposals_close_at` and nothing else. Sci, 2026-10-06: *"The sort can be by Value (Asc/Desc); By Time (prazo)."* **The work is the keyset, not the `order by`**: `sortKey()` is one definition read by both the order and the cursor comparison, which is the rule the file already stated for `HALTED` — *a sort key that does not match its cursor silently skips rows at every page boundary*. Every component is non-null and ascending so the keyset stays a single row comparison: nulls-last is `(estimated_value is null)` as its own component, descending is negation. **Measured**: of 24 162 open tenders **19 574 (81%) carry a value and 4 588 do not**, so they sort last in both directions or they own the first page of *menor valor*. `estimated_value` is `numeric(16,2)` — the hazard is the cursor, where node-postgres hands numerics over as text, so `cursorKey` casts `::numeric` and the value is regex-validated on decode. The cursor is tagged with its order and a mismatch **restarts the run**, deliberately: a restart can only re-show rows the reader has seen, while reading a cursor under the wrong key hides rows and says nothing. `deadline` keeps its exact legacy three-field format, so a page-2 request in flight across the deploy still lands where it meant to. The control is three real `<a href>`s in a `<details>` — it works before hydration, stays outside the filter `<summary>` (WCAG 4.1.2), and loses only `pointer-events-none`, because a control must receive its own clicks. **Review found an elected tab being promoted to a chosen one by every sort link — and then that the filter form's `onSubmit` had been doing the same thing on `main` all along**, while the hidden `group` field beside it was gated on `groupChosen`: the two halves of one form disagreed, so changing the UF pinned a tab the reader never chose and `bestGroup()` could no longer help. Both read the flag now. lint 0 errors, typecheck clean, 1 705 passed / 30 skipped, 8 journeys, three mutation checks each restored byte-identically. Follow-up: **no index on `tenders.estimated_value`** — not measured and no number guessed |
| 2026-10-06 | D52 (filter the Radar by modalidade and by ME/EPP) | in review | https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/246 | **Based on `task/d51-sort` (#245) — merge that first, then confirm this PR's base retargeted to `main` before merging it** (#221 was merged into a stale base 38 seconds after #220 in this repo, and the card it carried was absent from `main` afterwards). Sci, 2026-10-06: *"The filter can be by: Type: Dispensa, Pregao and the other methods; Exclusive or Not exclusive to ME/EPP"*. **Modalidade filters `modality_id`, not the text beside it** — and the first commit filtered the name, which the review caught: `modality_name` arrives from **two different PNCP endpoints under two different field names**, stored verbatim, so one re-worded hyphen turns the option into an empty screen while the Radar says nothing is open (`memory: empty-result-is-not-absence`). Measured 2026-10-06 18:19 UTC in one snapshot: **58 495 rows, 0 null `modality_id`**; of 24 763 open, Pregão 17 259 · Concorrência 4 016 · Dispensa 3 488. The three are hardcoded, which is safe **only because *Todas* adds no predicate** — the sweep's modality set comes from a job payload and `_MODALITY_NAMES` knows ten, so an `in (…)` default would hide a fourth from every reader with nothing failing. **ME/EPP: Sci ruled that *Exclusivo* holds `mixed` too** — 1 810 open editais were under a heading saying *Não exclusivo* while their own cards said *"Exclusivos e cotas ME/EPP"*; what stays is `quota` (tag *"Cotas ME/EPP"*), `none` and the silent rows, and a cota is not exclusivity. Both clauses of the negative are `is distinct from`, never `not in`, which would be `unknown` for the **2 086** silent rows and drop 8.4% of the open Radar out of both buckets. The two options partition exactly: **6 229 + 18 534 = 24 763**, counted against Postgres. Both conditions live in **`scope()`**, the function the list and the counts share, so a tab can never read `Compatíveis 13` above a page of 4. **Layout**: five controls arranged by a container query, because at a 1024px window with the shell's 264px rail out this form is **720px, not 1024** — and the discriminating Playwright case is the rail **collapsed**, which no media query can observe. lint 0 errors, typecheck clean, 1 753 passed / 31 skipped, 28 journeys. | 2026-10-06 | D54 (every assertion in `radar.db.test.ts` scoped to rows this run wrote) + D63/D64 carded | in review | https://github.com/Scintechn/licitaqui/pull/252 | **`RUN_ID` scopes what a suite writes and cannot scope what it reads**, and several assertions here read the whole table — the sharpest asserted its own tender was **on page 1 of 20** of a search filtered by neither agency nor run. The handle is one run-scoped lexeme appended to the fixtures' **search vector**, because `?q=` is the only run-scoped filter the route exposes; where scoping would have *weakened* an assertion it was left unscoped and argued instead (two lower bounds a foreign row can only raise; one `not.toContain` that is direction-safe). **Proved against interference written on purpose**, because R1's orphans were already deleted and 30 runs against an empty table prove nothing: 80 foreign open tenders with deadlines ten days earlier than any fixture, half carrying the compatible segment, plus a writer inserting a visitor and two jobs every 2 s. **The file as it was fails 7 of 35; as it is, 30 consecutive green runs**, md5-checked before each one. **The §4b review found three blockers in replacements that were already green** — the visitor traces missed `readViewer → readOrCreateViewer`, a one-word change that inserts a row escaping both the fingerprint and cookie traces while the old global count caught it; two assertions keyed on constants that `jobs_dedupe` would satisfy **for ever** after the first firing; and a key-scoped sweep assertion that cannot see an invented key. **A measured negative result worth keeping**: a watermark over `jobs.id` + kind does **not** work — a kind and an id say a job is new, never who made it. Two defects the file was hiding: *"accents and all"* took the longest word (`componentes`, no diacritic), so the `pt_unaccent` path was asserted by an ASCII word matching itself; and `newVisitor(null)` rows were **never deleted**, which is one of the ways R1 came to hold orphans. **D63 is the bigger finding**: `TEST_DATABASE_URL_R2` and `_D4` **do not exist**, and `?? testDatabaseUrl()` hides it, so `sort`, `pagination`, `comparables`, `entitlement` and `quota-spent` all run in the shared `licitaqui_test` while naming a database of their own — measured separately at 207 tenders, 157 visitors, 1 061 jobs of accumulated orphans. lint 0 errors, typecheck clean, 1 796 passed / 32 skipped with the interference still present |

One polite live region now owns the sentence: in the DOM **empty on every paint** (a region *inserted* carrying its text is announced unreliably) and a **sibling** of the list's `@container`, because `container-type: inline-size` is a containing block for `position: fixed` — nested inside it would anchor to the bottom of the grid, off screen. **D57 needed no new string**: `aria-labelledby` composes each name from the button's own `sr-only` action word and the card's existing title, with `tenderTitleId` derived on both sides so the reference cannot dangle. **The old e2e was worse than tolerating the defect** — it counted `{ name: 'Favoritar' }` and so *required* the names to be identical, and **would have gone on passing after the fix**, because Playwright matches `name` as a substring.
